The smoothing effect provided by this lemma will be crucial in establishing global regular solutions and it is actually the reason why we need to consider
weighted
spaces.
Indeed, the regularisation effect of the time-weight is the crucial ingredient of the argument. This will be clear in the last part of the proof.

Proof.

We mostly follow the argument of
[
23
, Lemma 3.5]
, which in turn is inspired by the one of
[
37
, Lemma 4.1]
.

Now fix
t_{1}<t_{2}\in(0,t^{+})
and
\tau=t_{2}-t_{1}
. Given
\boldsymbol{\uppsi}
maximal solution to (
3.1
), we define
\boldsymbol{\upzeta}(t):=\boldsymbol{\uppsi}(t+t_{1})
for any
t\in(0,\tau)
. Then, since
\boldsymbol{\uppsi}\in E_{2,1}(0,t^{+})
, we have that
\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\in E_{2,1}(0,\tau)
. Moreover, we recall that
\boldsymbol{\uppsi}\in E_{2,1}(0,t^{+})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{0}(0,t^{+};W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega))
by (
3.1
) with
\rho=1
, hence this implies that

\lVert\boldsymbol{\uppsi}(t_{2})\rVert_{W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega)}=\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}(\tau)\rVert_{W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\tau}\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert_{E_{2,1}(0,\tau)}\leq C_{\tau}\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert_{E_{2,\rho}(0,\tau)},

(3.11)

where the last inequality holds for any
\rho\in(1/p,1]
, due to Remark
3.1
. Note that here we passed from the unweighted case to the weighted one, in order to exploit the time-regularisation effect.
Now, observe that, by definition,
\boldsymbol{\upzeta}=\binom{\xi}{\theta}
satisfies the following system of partial differential equations:

\displaystyle\xi_{t}-(AF^{\prime\prime}(\xi)+Ba)\Delta\xi+\chi\Delta\theta

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=AF^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)\nabla\xi\cdot\nabla\xi+2B\nabla a\cdot\nabla\xi+B\Delta a\xi-\divergence(B\nabla J\ast\xi)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+P(\xi)(\theta+\chi(1-\xi)-AF^{\prime}(\xi)-Ba\xi+BJ\ast\xi+\chi\theta)-\mathbbm{h}(\xi)u

\displaystyle\hbox{in $Q_{\tau}$},

\displaystyle\theta_{t}-\Delta\theta+\chi\Delta\xi=-P(\xi)(\theta+\chi(1-\xi)-AF^{\prime}(\xi)-Ba\xi+BJ\ast\xi+\chi\theta)+v

\displaystyle\hbox{in $Q_{\tau}$},

\displaystyle\nabla\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}=-l(x,\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega})(B\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega}\nabla a\cdot\boldsymbol{n}-B(\nabla J\ast\xi)_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}-\chi\nabla\theta_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n})

\displaystyle\hbox{on $\Sigma_{\tau}$},

\displaystyle\nabla\theta_{\mid\partial\Omega}=\chi\nabla\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega}

\displaystyle\hbox{on $\Sigma_{\tau}$},

\displaystyle\xi(0)=\varphi(t_{1}),\hskip 10.00002pt\theta(0)=\sigma(t_{1})

\displaystyle\hbox{in $\Omega$}.

At this point, we assume
\boldsymbol{\upzeta}
fixed and consider the following non-homogeneous
linear
parabolic problem for
\boldsymbol{w}=\binom{w}{z}
:

\displaystyle w_{t}-(AF^{\prime\prime}(\xi)+Ba)\Delta w+\chi\Delta z

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=AF^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)\nabla\xi\cdot\nabla\xi+2B\nabla a\cdot\nabla\xi+B\Delta a\xi-\divergence(B\nabla J\ast\xi)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+P(\xi)(\theta+\chi(1-\xi)-AF^{\prime}(\xi)-Ba\xi+BJ\ast\xi+\chi\theta)-\mathbbm{h}(\xi)u

\displaystyle\hbox{in $Q_{\tau}$},

\displaystyle z_{t}-\Delta z+\chi\Delta w=-P(\xi)(\theta+\chi(1-\xi)-AF^{\prime}(\xi)-Ba\xi+BJ\ast\xi+\chi\theta)+v

\displaystyle\hbox{in $Q_{\tau}$},

\displaystyle\nabla w_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}-\chi l(x,\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega})\nabla z_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}=-l(x,\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega})(B\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega}\nabla a\cdot\boldsymbol{n}-B(\nabla J\ast\xi)_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n})

\displaystyle\hbox{on $\Sigma_{\tau}$},

\displaystyle\nabla z_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}-\chi\nabla w_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}=0

\displaystyle\hbox{on $\Sigma_{\tau}$},

\displaystyle w(0)=\varphi(t_{1}),\hskip 10.00002ptz(0)=\sigma(t_{1})

\displaystyle\hbox{in $\Omega$},

where we observe that, since
l(x,\xi)^{-1}=AF^{\prime\prime}(\xi)+Ba(x)\geq c_{0}>\chi^{2}\geq 0
by
A3
, the differential operator

-\left(\begin{pmatrix}AF^{\prime\prime}(\xi)+Ba(x)&-\chi\\
-\chi&1\end{pmatrix}\binom{\Delta w}{\Delta z}\right)

and the boundary operator

\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&-\chi\,l(x,\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega})\\
-\chi&1\end{pmatrix}\binom{\nabla w\cdot\boldsymbol{n}}{\nabla z\cdot\boldsymbol{n}}\right)

are normally elliptic and satisfy the Lopatinskii-Shapiro type condition. Again, both conditions can be verified by applying
[
2
, Theorem 4.4 and Example 4.5]
. Moreover, by the embedding (
3.1
), it also follows that
\lVert l(\cdot,\xi(\cdot))^{-1}\rVert_{\mathcal{BUC}(\overline{Q_{T}})}\leq C
, where
C
depends on the fact that
\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{0}([t_{1},t_{2}];\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}))}\leq\delta_{\beta}
for any fixed
\beta\in(0,1)
. Now, we know that
\boldsymbol{v}=\boldsymbol{\upzeta}
is a solution of this problem, then we can apply the linear maximal parabolic regularity result
[
39
, Theorem 2.1]
to infer that there exists a constant
C>0
, depending on the parameters of the system, on
\tau
and
\delta_{\beta}
, such that the norm of
\boldsymbol{\upzeta}
is bounded by the corresponding norms of the right-hand side, the boundary data and the initial data, namely:

\begin{split}&\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}\leq C\Big(\lVert F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)\nabla\xi\cdot\nabla\xi\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}+\lVert\nabla a\cdot\nabla\xi\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}+\lVert\Delta a\xi\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}\\
&\qquad+\lVert\divergence(B\nabla J\ast\xi)\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}+\lVert\mathbbm{h}(\xi)u\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}+\lVert v\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}\\
&\qquad+\lVert P(\xi)(\theta+\chi(1-\xi)-AF^{\prime}(\xi)-Ba\xi+BJ\ast\xi+\chi\theta)\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}\Big)\\
&\quad+C\Big(\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(B\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega}\nabla a\cdot\boldsymbol{n})\rVert_{F_{\rho}(I)}+\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(B(\nabla J\ast\xi)_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n})\rVert_{F_{\rho}(I)}\Big)\\
&\quad+C\lVert\boldsymbol{\uppsi}(t_{1})\rVert_{W^{2(\rho-1/p),p}(\Omega)^{2}},\end{split}

(3.12)

where, from here onward, we use the notation
I:=(0,\tau)
.
Observe that
linear
maximal regularity, as in
[
39
, Theorem 2.1]
, works for any
\rho\in(1/p,1]
. This is important, since in the end we need some freedom in choosing
\rho
small enough.
We are now left to estimate each term on the right-hand side of (
3.12
).

Here we need a generalisation of Gagliardo-Nirenberg’s inequality for fractional Sobolev spaces, that was stated and proved in
[
3
]
. Indeed, we apply
[
3
, Proposition 4.1]
with
\theta=1/2
,
p=2q\in(1,+\infty)
,
p_{1}=r\in(1,+\infty)
,
p_{2}=q
,
s_{1}=\alpha
and
s_{2}=\gamma>0
such that

1-\frac{N}{2q}<\frac{1}{2}\left(\alpha-\frac{N}{r}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\gamma-\frac{N}{q}\right),

to have the following inequality

\lVert u\rVert_{W^{1,2q}(\Omega)}\leq C\lVert u\rVert^{1/2}_{W^{\alpha,r}(\Omega)}\lVert u\rVert^{1/2}_{W^{\gamma,q}(\Omega)},

(3.13)

where we can choose
q\in(1,+\infty)
,
r>>1
,
\alpha\in(0,\beta)
and
\gamma<2
. Furthermore, we also need another version of Gagliardo-Nirenberg’s inequality for fractional Sobolev spaces, this time from
[
7
]
. Indeed, we can apply
[
7
, Theorem 1]
with
s_{1}=\alpha\in(0,\beta)
,
s_{2}=2
,
s=\gamma\in(1,2)
,
p=p_{1}=p_{2}=q
and
\delta\in(0,1)
such that
\gamma=\alpha\delta+2(1-\delta)
, to obtain the inequality

\lVert u\rVert_{W^{\gamma,q}(\Omega)}\leq C\lVert u\rVert^{\delta}_{W^{\alpha,q}(\Omega)}\lVert u\rVert^{1-\delta}_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}.

(3.14)

Then, we can begin estimating the terms on the right-hand side of (
3.12
). Starting from the first one, we can infer that

\displaystyle\lVert F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)\nabla\xi\cdot\nabla\xi\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}^{p}=\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)\lvert\nabla\xi\rvert^{2}\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lVert F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{Q_{T}})}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{2p}_{L^{2p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{2p}_{W^{1,2p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Then, to estimate
\lVert\xi\rVert^{2p}_{W^{1,2p}(\Omega)}
, we first use (
3.13
) with
q=p
, then the embedding
\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{\alpha,r}(\Omega)
for
\alpha\in(0,\beta)
and
r\in(1,+\infty)
and finally (
3.14
) with
p=q
, together with Young’s inequality with exponents
1/(1-\delta)
and
1/\delta
and again the embedding
\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{\alpha,p}(\Omega)
. Indeed, we have that

\displaystyle\lVert F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)\nabla\xi\cdot\nabla\xi\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{2p}_{W^{1,2p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{\alpha,r}(\Omega)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{\gamma,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{\gamma,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p(1-\delta)}_{W^{2,p}(\Omega)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p\delta}_{W^{\alpha,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{2,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t+C_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}+C_{\varepsilon},

for some
\varepsilon>0
, depending on the parameters of the system,
\tau
,
p
and
\delta_{\beta}
, to be chosen later.
Going on with the second term of (
3.12
), by using Gagliardo-Nirenberg and Young’s inequalities to say that

\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\varepsilon\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{2,p}(\Omega)}+C_{\varepsilon}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)},

we can estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla a\cdot\nabla\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\nabla a\rVert^{p}_{\infty}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}\leq(b^{*})^{p}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{2,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t+C_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}+C_{\varepsilon}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(0,\tau;\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega))}\leq\varepsilon\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}+C_{\varepsilon},

where again
\varepsilon
is to be chosen later.
For the third and fourth term, we use hypothesis
B1
, together with Remark
3.7
, and similar techniques to say that

\displaystyle\lVert\Delta a\,\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}+\lVert\divergence(\nabla J\ast\xi)\rVert^{p}_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\Delta a\rVert^{p}_{L^{2p}(\Omega)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{2p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t+2b_{p}^{p}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{2,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t+C_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\varepsilon\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}+C_{\varepsilon}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(0,\tau;\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega))}\leq\varepsilon\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}+C_{\varepsilon},

where we used that
a\in W^{2,2p}(\Omega)
by Remark
3.7
and the embedding
W^{1,p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{2p}(\Omega)
, which surely holds if
p>N+2
.
Then, we can easily see that for the fifth and sixth terms we have:

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbbm{h}(\xi)u\rVert^{p}_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}\leq\lVert u\rVert^{p}_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\lVert\mathbbm{h}(\xi)\rVert^{p}_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}\leq C,

\displaystyle\lVert u\rVert_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}\leq\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C,

since
u,v\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
and
\mathbbm{h}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.
Moreover, for the seventh one, by similar methods, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\lVert P(\xi)(\theta+\chi(1-\xi)-AF^{\prime}(\xi)-Ba\xi+BJ\ast\xi+\chi\theta)\rVert^{p}_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lVert P\rVert_{\infty}^{p}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\theta+\chi(1-\xi)-AF^{\prime}(\xi)-Ba\xi+BJ\ast\xi+\chi\theta\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\left(\lVert\theta\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+\lVert F^{\prime}(\xi)\rVert^{p}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+1\right)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t+C_{\tau}\lVert F^{\prime}(\xi)\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{Q_{T}})}+C_{\tau}\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t+C_{\delta_{\beta}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert^{p/2}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert^{p/2}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t+C_{\delta_{\beta}}\leq\varepsilon\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert^{p}_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}+C_{\varepsilon}.

We are now left only with the boundary terms, for which we first have to recall some results about embeddings for trace spaces. Indeed, by
[
38
, Theorem 4.5]
, we have that the trace operator
\Tr_{\Omega}:u\mapsto u_{\mid\partial\Omega}
is continuous between the spaces

\Tr_{\Omega}:W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))\cap L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))\to F_{\rho}(I),

with an embedding constant depending on
\tau
. In this way, we can estimate the norm on the boundary space
F_{\rho}(I)
with a norm on a functional space on the whole
\Omega
, i.e.

\lVert f\rVert_{F_{\rho}(I)}\leq C\lVert f\rVert_{W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))\cap L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}.

Moreover, we recall that the function
l(x,\cdot)
is in
\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
for a.e.
x\in\Omega
, therefore, since
\xi\in E_{2,\rho}(I)
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
, we have that

\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}+\lVert l^{\prime}(\cdot,\xi)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}.

Then, we can estimate the eighth and ninth term in (
3.12
) as

\begin{split}&\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(B\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega}\nabla a\cdot\boldsymbol{n})\rVert^{p}_{F_{\rho}(I)}+\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(B(\nabla J\ast\xi)_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n})\rVert^{p}_{F_{\rho}(I)}\\
&\quad\leq C(\tau)\Big(\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(\xi\nabla a)\rVert^{p}_{W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))}+\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(\nabla J\ast\xi)\rVert^{p}_{W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(\xi\nabla a)\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}+\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(\nabla J\ast\xi)\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}\Big).\end{split}

(3.15)

For the first two terms, we use the intrinsic norm of fractional Sobolev spaces and hypothesis
A2
, together with the mean value theorem and the bounds on
l(\cdot,\xi)
, to infer that

\displaystyle\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(\xi\nabla a)\rVert^{p}_{W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{t^{p(1-\rho)}}{(s-t)^{1+(1/2)p}}\lVert l(\cdot,\xi(s))\xi(s)\nabla a-l(\cdot,\xi(t))\xi(t)\nabla a\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{t^{p(1-\rho)}}{(s-t)^{1+(1/2)p}}\lVert(l(\cdot,\xi(s))-l(\cdot,\xi(t)))\xi(s)\nabla a\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{t^{p(1-\rho)}}{(s-t)^{1+(1/2)p}}\lVert l(\cdot,\xi(t))(\xi(s)-\xi(t))\nabla a\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{t^{p(1-\rho)}}{(s-t)^{1+(1/2)p}}(b^{\ast})^{p}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega)}\lVert(l(\cdot,\xi(s))-l(\cdot,\xi(t)))\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{t^{p(1-\rho)}}{(s-t)^{1+(1/2)p}}(b^{\ast})^{p}\lVert l(\cdot,\xi(t))\rVert^{p}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lVert\xi(s)-\xi(t)\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))},

where, for the last inequality, we used the local Lipschitz continuity of the function
l(x,\cdot)
. In a similar way, by using linearity of the convolution and Young’s inequality for convolutions, together with hypothesis
A2
, one can easily see that also

\displaystyle\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(\nabla J\ast\xi)\rVert^{p}_{W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{t^{p(1-\rho)}}{(s-t)^{1+(1/2)p}}\lVert l(\cdot,\xi(s))\nabla J\ast\xi(s)-l(\cdot,\xi(t))\nabla J\ast\xi(t)\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))}.

Next, by recalling that
W^{1,p}(\Omega)
is a Banach algebra if
p>N
and by using
B1
to say that
a\in W^{2,q}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{1}(\overline{\Omega})
for
q>N
, we can also estimate the last two terms with similar techniques, namely

\displaystyle\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(\xi\nabla a)\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(0,\tau;\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}))}\lVert\xi\nabla a\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}+\lVert\xi\nabla a\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(0,\tau;\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}))}\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\left(\lVert\xi\nabla a\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+\lVert\nabla\xi\otimes\nabla a\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{2p}(\Omega)}\lVert D^{2}a\rVert^{p}_{L^{2p}(\Omega)}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C_{\delta_{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\left(\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+\lVert l^{\prime}(\cdot,\xi)\nabla\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t+C_{\delta_{\beta}}+C_{\delta_{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\tau}t^{p(1-\rho)}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}\left(1+\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}\right),

where we used Hölder’s inequality and the embedding
W^{1,p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{2p}(\Omega)
.
Analogously, by using
B1
, Remark
3.7
, Young’s convolution inequality and the same reasoning as above, we also see that

\displaystyle\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(\nabla J\ast\xi)\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(0,\tau;\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}))}\lVert\nabla J\ast\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}+\lVert\nabla J\ast\xi\rVert^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}(0,\tau;\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}))}\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}\left(1+\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}\right).

Then, by putting all together, (
3.15
) now becomes

\displaystyle\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(B\xi_{\mid\partial\Omega}\nabla a\cdot\boldsymbol{n})\rVert^{p}_{F_{\rho}(I)}+\lVert l(\cdot,\xi)(B(\nabla J\ast\xi)_{\mid\partial\Omega}\cdot\boldsymbol{n})\rVert^{p}_{F_{\rho}(I)}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C_{\delta_{\beta}}\left(1+\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))\cap L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}\right).

At this point, observe that

E_{2,\rho}(I)\hookrightarrow W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))\cap L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))\hookrightarrow E_{0,\rho}(I),

in particular, one can see that, by interpolation theory (cf.
[
35
]
), we actually have that

W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))\cap L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))=[E_{2,\rho}(I),E_{0,\rho}(I)]_{1/2},

then, by using also Young’s inequality, it follows that

\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{W^{1/2,p}_{\rho}(I,L^{p}(\Omega))\cap L^{p}_{\rho}(I,W^{1,p}(\Omega))}\leq\varepsilon\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}+C_{\varepsilon}\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{0,\rho}(I)}\leq\varepsilon\lVert\xi\rVert^{p}_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}+C_{\varepsilon}(\tau,\delta_{\beta}).

Now we can finally group together all the results we obtained in estimating the right-hand side of (
3.12
), to deduce that

\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}\leq\varepsilon\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}+C_{\varepsilon}+C\lVert\boldsymbol{\uppsi}(t_{1})\rVert_{W^{2(\rho-1/p),p}(\Omega)^{2}}.

Then, by choosing
\varepsilon>0
, depending on
\tau
,
p
,
\delta_{\beta}
and the parameters of the system, small enough, we can infer that

\lVert\boldsymbol{\upzeta}\rVert_{E_{2,\rho}(I)}\leq C\left(1+\lVert\boldsymbol{\uppsi}(t_{1})\rVert_{W^{2(\rho-1/p),p}(\Omega)^{2}}\right),

(3.16)

up to renaming the constants.

Hence, by combining (
3.16
) with (
3.11
), we find that

\lVert\boldsymbol{\uppsi}(t_{2})\rVert_{W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(1+\lVert\boldsymbol{\uppsi}(t_{1})\rVert_{W^{2(\rho-1/p),p}(\Omega)^{2}}\right).

As already anticipated, here is where the regularising effect of the temporal weight
\rho
comes into play. Indeed, we are now free to choose any
\rho\in(1/p,1]
and we actually take
\rho<1
by asking that
\rho=1/p+\varepsilon
, for some
\varepsilon<\min\{1-1/p,\beta/2\}
. In this way, the fractional exponent
(2(\rho-1/p))
is strictly smaller than
\beta
. Therefore, we can use the embedding
\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\overline{\Omega})\hookrightarrow W^{\alpha,p}(\Omega)
, which holds for any
\alpha\in(0,\beta)
and any
p\in(1,+\infty)
, to finally deduce the inequality

\lVert\boldsymbol{\uppsi}(t_{2})\rVert_{(W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega))^{2}}\leq C(1+\lVert\boldsymbol{\uppsi}(t_{1})\rVert_{(\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega))^{2}}).

This concludes the proof of Lemma
3.11
.
∎

4
Strong global well-posedness

The next step in establishing global high regularity results for the solution to (
1.2
)-(
1.6
), is to extend the maximal regularity found in the previous section to the whole time-interval
(0,T)
. To do this, we first need an
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
-estimate for both
\varphi
and
\sigma
, which is achieved respectively by an Alikakos-Moser iterative scheme and by parabolic regularity theory. Here it is necessary to put the chemotaxis parameter
\chi
equal to
0
, in order to make these arguments work. The main problem due to chemotaxis is the treatment of the cross-diffusion terms
-\chi\sigma
in (
1.3
) and
+\chi\Delta\varphi
in (
1.4
), which essentially prevent the
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
-estimate, if starting only from the low regularity given by weak solutions. Indeed, we recall that the only global result available up to now is the one of Theorem
2.1
; therefore we are forced to rely on that to prove global boundedness of the solutions. Subsequently, we prove Hölder-type estimates on the solutions, which, in conjunction to Lemma
3.11
, allow us to extend the maximal regularity to the whole
(0,T)
, if
\chi=0
. This is then enough to prove a continuous dependence result in strong spaces, which will be the starting point of the subsequent study of the optimal control problem.

4.1
Global maximal regularity

From now on, we consider the system of equations with
\chi=0
, namely the system (
1.7
)–(
1.11
), which we recall here for convenience:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\varphi-\Delta\mu=P(\varphi)(\sigma-\mu)-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)u\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

\displaystyle\mu=AF^{\prime}(\varphi)+Ba\varphi-BJ\ast\varphi\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\sigma-\Delta\sigma=-P(\varphi)(\sigma-\mu)+v\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T}.

\displaystyle\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\mu=\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\sigma=0\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{on }\Sigma_{T},

\displaystyle\varphi(0)=\varphi_{0},\hskip 10.00002pt\sigma(0)=\sigma_{0}\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }\Omega.

First, we state and prove the results about the
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
-estimates for
\varphi
and
\sigma
. Note that in both cases, we only need the weak regularity together with stronger assumptions on some data of the system. In particular, we have to further assume that:

B7.

P\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.

With this extra hypothesis we can now prove the following result.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume hypotheses
A1
-
A9
,
B7
and let
\varphi_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
. Let
(\varphi,\mu,\sigma)
be a weak solution to (
1.7
)–(
1.11
), with regularities given by Theorem
2.1
.

Then, there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the data of the system, such that

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C.

Proof.

We perform an Alikakos-Moser iteration scheme, by taking some inspiration from
[
4
, Theorem 2.1]
. We start by testing (
1.7
) by
\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}
, with
p>1
. Note that, for this to be rigorous, one should have to consider truncated versions for the test function, i.e.
\varphi_{\lambda}\lvert\varphi_{\lambda}\rvert^{p-1}
with
\varphi_{\lambda}=\max\{\min\{\varphi,\lambda\},-\lambda\}
for any
\lambda>0
. In this way,
\varphi_{\lambda}\lvert\varphi_{\lambda}\rvert^{p-1}
would be eligible as a test function in the weak formulation. Then, one can proceed with all the estimates below and pass to the limit as
\lambda\to+\infty
to get the result. Here, with the idea of not overburdening the exposition, we proceed formally. Then, we have:

\langle\varphi_{t},\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}\rangle_{V}+(\nabla\mu,\nabla(\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}))_{H}=(P(\varphi)(\sigma-\mu),\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1})_{H}-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)u,\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1})_{H}.

From now on, we will call the reaction term
R:=\sigma-\mu
for simplicity and recall that by Theorem
2.1

\lVert R\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}\leq C.

(4.1)

Then, we rewrite the previous identity by using the explicit expression of
\mu
and we get:

\begin{split}&\langle\varphi_{t},\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}\rangle_{V}+((AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba)\nabla\varphi,\nabla(\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}))_{H}\\
&\quad=-B(\nabla a\,\varphi,\nabla(\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}))_{H}+B(\nabla J\ast\varphi,\nabla(\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}))_{H}\\
&\qquad+(P(\varphi)R,\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1})_{H}-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)u,\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1})_{H}.\end{split}

(4.2)

Before going on, we also recall the following useful identities, which hold for any function
f:Q_{T}\to\mathbb{R}
sufficiently regular and any
p>1
:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lvert f\rvert^{p+1}=(p+1)f_{t}\,f\lvert f\rvert^{p-1},

(4.3)

\displaystyle\nabla(f\lvert f\rvert^{p-1})=p\lvert f\rvert^{p-1}\nabla f,

(4.4)

\displaystyle\nabla(\lvert f\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}})=\frac{p+1}{2}\lvert f\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\sgn(f)\nabla f.

(4.5)

Now we treat all the terms in (
4.2
) one by one. Starting from the first one, by using (
4.3
) (which can be extended to the setting of Hilbert triplets), we deduce that

\langle\varphi_{t},\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}\rangle_{V}=\frac{1}{p+1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Next, by using (
4.4
) and (
4.5
), together with
A3
, we deduce that

\displaystyle((AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba)\nabla\varphi,\nabla(\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}))_{H}=p\int_{\Omega}(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba)\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}\nabla\varphi\cdot\nabla\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=p\int_{\Omega}(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba)\lvert\nabla\varphi\rvert^{2}(\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\geq pc_{0}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\nabla\varphi\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{4c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Hence, we are left to estimate the terms on the right-hand side of (
4.2
). Here we will repeatedly use the trivial identity
\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p}=\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}
to simplify some calculations. Indeed, by using
A2
, (
4.4
), (
4.5
) and Cauchy-Schwarz and Young’s inequalities, we have that

\displaystyle-B(\nabla a\,\varphi,\nabla(\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}))_{H}\leq Bb^{\ast}\lvert(\varphi,\nabla(\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}))_{H}\rvert

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq Bb^{\ast}\int_{\Omega}p\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p}\lvert\nabla\varphi\rvert\,\mathrm{d}x=Bb^{\ast}p\int_{\Omega}(\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\lvert\nabla\varphi\rvert)\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq Bb^{\ast}p\left(\frac{4}{(p+1)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+C(p+1)\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Then, for the convolution term, we use a similar strategy, by exploiting (
4.4
), (
4.5
), Cauchy-Schwarz and Young’s inequalities, Hölder’s inequality with
\frac{p-1}{p+1}+\frac{2}{p+1}=1
and Young’s inequality for convolutions. Indeed, we can infer that

\displaystyle B(\nabla J\ast\varphi,\nabla(\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}))_{H}=Bp\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla J\ast\varphi\rvert\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}\lvert\nabla\varphi\rvert\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=Bp\int_{\Omega}\left(\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\lvert\nabla\varphi\rvert\right)\left(\lvert\nabla J\ast\varphi\rvert\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{c_{0}p}{8}\int_{\Omega}\left(\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\lvert\nabla\varphi\rvert\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+Cp\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1}\lvert\nabla J\ast\varphi\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\frac{1}{2}\frac{c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+Cp\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p+1}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla J\ast\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{2}{p+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+Cp\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p+1}}(b^{\ast})^{2}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{2}{p+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+C(p+1)\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Moreover, for the reaction term, we use
B7
and a combination of the generalised Hölder’s inequality with
\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{6}+\frac{2}{3}=1
and Young’s inequality, yielding

\displaystyle(P(\varphi)R,\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1})_{H}\leq\int_{\Omega}\lvert P(\varphi)\rvert\lvert R\rvert\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\lVert P\rVert_{\infty}\int_{\Omega}\lvert R\rvert\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lVert R\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\varepsilon\frac{c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+C_{\varepsilon}\frac{(p+1)^{2}}{4c_{0}p}\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\varepsilon\frac{c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+C_{\varepsilon}(p+1)\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}},

where we used Young’s inequality with
\delta=\varepsilon\frac{c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}
and
\varepsilon>0
, independent of
p
, to be chosen later.
Finally, for the last term we argue exactly as above, by recalling that
u\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
and using hypothesis
A7
. Indeed, we have that:

\displaystyle(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)u,\varphi\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p-1})_{H}\leq\lVert h\rVert_{\infty}\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\lvert\Omega\rvert^{1/6}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\varepsilon\frac{c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+C_{\varepsilon}(p+1)\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}},

where
\varepsilon>0
, independent of
p
, is again to be chosen later.
Therefore, starting from (
4.2
), we arrived at

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{p+1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{3c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\quad\leq\varepsilon\frac{c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+C(p+1)\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\qquad+C_{\varepsilon}(p+1)\left(1+\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}.\end{split}

(4.6)

At this point, we observe that, by the Sobolev embedding
V\hookrightarrow L^{6}(\Omega)
, it follows that

\lVert\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rVert^{2}_{V}\geq\frac{1}{\tilde{C}}\lVert\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}=\frac{1}{\tilde{C}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}},

therefore by the definition of the norm in
V
, we can write that

\frac{3c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\nabla\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\geq\frac{3c_{0}p}{\tilde{C}(p+1)^{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}-\underbrace{\frac{3c_{0}p}{(p+1)^{2}}}_{\leq C(p+1)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\right).

Then, by choosing
\varepsilon=1/\tilde{C}
, (
4.6
) now becomes:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{p+1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{2c_{0}p}{\tilde{C}(p+1)^{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\\
&\quad\leq C(p+1)\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x+C(p+1)\left(1+\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}.\end{split}

(4.7)

Next, we want to estimate further the two integrals on the right-hand side, in order to get to an inequality starting from which it is easier to perform an Alikakos-Moser iteration. Indeed, by using Hölder’s inequality with
\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{6}+\frac{2}{3}=1
and Young’s inequality with
\varepsilon=\frac{c_{0}p}{C\tilde{C}\lvert\Omega\rvert^{1/6}(p+1)^{3}}
, we infer that

\displaystyle C(p+1)\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x=C(p+1)\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C(p+1)\lvert\Omega\rvert^{\frac{1}{6}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{c_{0}p}{\tilde{C}(p+1)^{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+\underbrace{C\frac{(p+1)^{4}}{p}}_{\leq C(p+1)^{3}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}.

Moreover, for the second term, we use Young’s inequality with exponents
\frac{p-1}{p+1}+\frac{2}{p+1}=1
and the fact that the function
x\mapsto x^{4/3}
is convex. Therefore, we get:

\displaystyle C(p+1)\left(1+\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C(p+1)\left(1+\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\left(\frac{p-1}{p+1}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{2}{p+1}\lvert\Omega\rvert\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C(p+1)\left(1+\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\left(\frac{p-1}{p+1}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}+\frac{2}{p+1}\lvert\Omega\rvert^{\frac{4}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C(p+1)\underbrace{\frac{p-1}{p+1}}_{\leq 1}\left(1+\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}+C\left(1+\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right).

For simplicity, we now call

g(t):=1+\lVert R\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\in L^{1}(0,T),

which is integrable due to Theorem
2.1
.
Consequently, starting from (
4.7
), we deduce the following inequality:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p+1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{c_{0}p}{\tilde{C}(p+1)^{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\left((p+1)^{3}+(p+1)g(t)\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}+Cg(t),

which, by multiplying everything by
(p+1)
becomes:

\begin{split}&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{c_{0}p}{\tilde{C}(p+1)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{3(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\\
&\quad\leq C\left((p+1)^{4}+(p+1)^{2}g(t)\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}+C(p+1)g(t).\end{split}

(4.8)

Now notice that the second term on the left-hand side is non-negative, therefore we can ignore it and consider the inequality:

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\left((p+1)^{4}+(p+1)^{2}g(t)\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}+C(p+1)g(t).

At this point, we can integrate on
(0,t)
, for any
t\in(0,T)
, and use (
4.1
), together with the embedding
V\hookrightarrow L^{6}(\Omega)
, to deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi(t)\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi_{0}\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x+C\int_{0}^{T}\left((p+1)^{4}+(p+1)^{2}g(t)\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C(p+1)\int_{0}^{T}g(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lvert\Omega\rvert\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert^{p+1}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+C(p+1)+C\underbrace{\left(T(p+1)^{4}+(p+1)^{2}\int_{0}^{T}g(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\right)}_{\leq C(p+1)^{4}}\sup_{(0,T)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lvert\Omega\rvert\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert^{p+1}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+C(p+1)+C(p+1)^{4}\sup_{(0,T)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C(p+1)^{4}\max\left\{\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert^{p+1}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)},1,\sup_{(0,T)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C(p+1)^{4}\max\left\{\max\{\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)},1\}^{p+1},\sup_{(0,T)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}\right\}.

Therefore, by taking the supremum on
(0,T)
also on the left-hand side, we arrive at the inequality:

\sup_{(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C(p+1)^{4}\max\left\{\max\{\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)},1\}^{p+1},\sup_{(0,T)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}(p+1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}\right\},

(4.9)

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
\Omega
,
T
, the parameters of the system and not on
p
.

We can now start the iteration scheme, by taking a sequence
\{p_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
p_{k}\to+\infty
as
k\to+\infty
, defined in the following way:

p_{0}=2,\hskip 10.00002ptp_{k+1}=\frac{4}{3}p_{k}\hskip 10.00002pt\forall k\in\mathbb{N}.

Then, by using
p=p_{k+1}-1>1
in (
4.9
) we get that

\sup_{(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p_{k+1}}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C(p_{k+1})^{4}\max\left\{\max\{\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)},1\}^{p_{k+1}},\sup_{(0,T)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{\frac{3}{4}p_{k+1}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}\right\}.

Hence, by calling
C_{0}=\max\{\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert_{\infty},1\}
and observing that
\frac{3}{4}p_{k+1}=p_{k}
, we further arrive at the inequality:

\sup_{(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p_{k+1}}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C(p_{k+1})^{4}\max\left\{C_{0}^{p_{k+1}},\sup_{(0,T)}\left(\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}\right\}.

(4.10)

Finally, we can apply
[
32
, Lemma A.1]
with

\displaystyle\delta_{0}=2,\hskip 10.00002pt\delta_{k}=p_{k}\hskip 10.00002pt\forall k\in\mathbb{N},\hskip 10.00002pta=\frac{4}{3}>1,\hskip 10.00002ptc=0,\hskip 10.00002ptb=4\geq 0

\displaystyle\gamma_{k}=\sup_{(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}x,\hskip 10.00002pt\gamma_{0}=\sup_{(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,

where
\gamma_{0}\leq C
by Theorem
2.1
, with
C
depending only on the parameters of the system. Indeed, by applying
[
32
, Lemma A.1]
, we can infer that

\gamma_{k}^{\frac{1}{\delta_{k}}}=\left(\sup_{(0,T)}\int_{\Omega}\lvert\varphi\rvert^{p_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{k}}}=\sup_{(0,T)}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{p_{k}}(\Omega)}\leq\overline{C},

with
\overline{C}
independent of
k
. Then, by sending
k\to+\infty
and recalling that, if
\lvert\Omega\rvert<+\infty
,
\lVert f\rVert_{p}\to\lVert f\rVert_{\infty}
as
p\to+\infty
for any
f
measurable, we obtain that

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

∎

Remark 4.2
.

Under the hypothesis of Proposition
4.1
, it also follows that there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the parameters of the system, such that

\lVert\mu\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C.

Indeed, from equation (
1.8
), since
F\in\mathcal{C}^{1}
and thus locally bounded, it follows that

\displaystyle\lVert\mu\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq A\lVert F^{\prime}(\varphi)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}+B\lVert a\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}+B\lVert J\rVert_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq A\lVert F^{\prime}(\varphi)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}+2Ba^{*}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C,

where we also used Young’s inequality for convolutions, together with hypothesis
A2
, and Proposition
4.1
.

Remark 4.3
.

Now that
\varphi\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
under the hypotheses of Proposition
4.1
, since
F\in\mathcal{C}^{4}(\mathbb{R})
,
P\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mathbbm{h}\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, by local boundedness we can say that there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the parameters of the system, such that

\lVert F^{(i)}(\varphi)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}+\lVert P^{(j)}(\varphi)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}+\lVert\mathbbm{h}^{(j)}(\varphi)\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for any $i=1,\dots,4$ and $j=0,1$.}

Proposition 4.4
.

Assume hypotheses
A1
-
A9
and
B7
. Let
\varphi_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
,
\sigma_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
and
v\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H)
. Let
(\varphi,\mu,\sigma)
be a weak solution to (
1.7
)–(
1.11
), with regularities given by Theorem
2.1
and Propositions
4.1
.

Then, there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the data of the system, such that

\lVert\sigma\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C.

Proof.

This is just an application of maximum principle for parabolic equations. Indeed, we rewrite equation (
1.9
) as

\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma=f_{\sigma},\hskip 10.00002pt\text{with }f_{\sigma}=P(\varphi)(\sigma-\mu)+v.

Then, we see that
f_{\sigma}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H)
uniformly with respect to the parameters, since
\varphi\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
by Proposition
4.1
,
\mu\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
by Remark
4.2
,
\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H)
by Theorem
2.1
and
v\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H)
by hypothesis. Then, we can apply
[
31
, Theorem 7.1, p. 181]
with
q=\infty
and
r=2
to conclude the proof.
∎

Next, we state and prove global Hölder-type estimates for both
\varphi
and
\sigma
.

Proposition 4.5
.

Assume
A1
-
A9
and
B7
. Let
\varphi_{0},\sigma_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
and
v\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
.
Let
(\varphi,\mu,\sigma)
be a weak solution to (
1.7
)–(
1.11
), with regularities given by Theorem
2.1
and Propositions
4.1
and
4.4
.

Then, there exist
\beta\in(0,1)
and a constant
C>0
, depending only on the parameters of the system, such that

\lvert\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,s)\rvert+\lvert\sigma(x,t)-\sigma(y,s)\rvert\leq C\left(\lvert x-y\rvert^{\beta}+\lvert t-s\rvert^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\right)\hskip 10.00002pt\forall(x,t),(y,s)\in\overline{\Omega}\times[0,T],

which means that
\varphi,\sigma\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta,\beta/2}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,T])
uniformly.

Proof.

The proof is inspired by
[
18
, Lemma 2]
and references therein, where the authors prove a similar estimate for a non-local Cahn-Hilliard equation with a convection term. The argument is heavily based on the results contained in
[
31
, Chapter II, Section 7]
.

Let
R>0
be such that

\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq R\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\lVert\sigma\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq R.

We start by proving the Hölder estimate for
\varphi
.
With the idea of applying
[
31
, Chapter II, Theorem 7.1]
, we let
k\in[0,R]
and
\zeta=\zeta(x,t)\in[0,1]
be a continuous piecewise smooth function, supported on space-time cylinders defined as
Q_{t_{0},t_{0}+\tau}(x_{0},\rho):=B_{\rho}(x_{0})\times(t_{0},t_{0}+\tau)
, where
B_{\rho}(x_{0})
is the open ball centred at
x_{0}
of radius
\rho>0
. Our aim is to prove an estimate like the one in
[
31
, Chapter II, Remark 7.2]
. Indeed, call
\varphi_{k}^{+}:=\max\{0,\varphi-k\}
, multiply equation (
1.7
) by
\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+}
and integrate over
Q_{t_{0},t}:=\Omega\times(t_{0},t)
, where
0\leq t_{0}<t<t_{0}+\tau\leq T
, to get:

\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\varphi_{t}\,\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\nabla\mu\cdot\nabla(\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s=\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(P(\varphi)(\sigma-\mu)-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)u)\,\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s.

By computing
\nabla\mu
, we can rewrite the previous equality as:

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\varphi_{t}\,\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba)\nabla\varphi\cdot\nabla(\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(\nabla a\,\varphi-\nabla J\ast\varphi)\cdot\nabla(\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s=\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(P(\varphi)(\sigma-\mu)-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)u)\,\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s.

Now, we consider each term one by one. By using the definition of
\varphi_{k}^{+}
and, in particular, the fact that
(\varphi_{k}^{+})_{t}=\varphi_{t}
on
\{\varphi>k\}
, we infer that

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\varphi_{t}\,\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s=\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(\varphi_{k}^{+})_{t}\,\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt=\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}((\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}\zeta^{2})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s-\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}\zeta\zeta_{t}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}[(\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}\zeta^{2}](t)\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}[(\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}\zeta^{2}](t_{0})\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}\zeta\zeta_{t}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s.

Next, by using the fact that
\nabla\varphi_{k}^{+}=\nabla\varphi
on
\{\varphi>k\}
and
A3
, we can estimate the second term from below in the following way:

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba)\nabla\varphi\cdot\nabla(\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s=\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba)\nabla\varphi_{k}^{+}\cdot\nabla(\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt=\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba)\lvert\nabla(\zeta\varphi_{k}^{+})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s-\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba)\lvert\nabla\zeta\rvert^{2}(\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\geq c_{0}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\lvert\nabla(\zeta\varphi_{k}^{+})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s-\underbrace{\lVert AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)+Ba\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}}_{\leq C_{R}}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\lvert\nabla\zeta\rvert^{2}(\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s.

Finally, we can also estimate the other two terms from above. Indeed, by using
A2
and Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(\nabla a\,\varphi-\nabla J\ast\varphi)\cdot\nabla(\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\lVert\nabla a\,\varphi-\nabla J\ast\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\lvert\nabla(\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+})\rvert\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\leq C_{R}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\left\lvert\zeta^{2}\nabla\varphi_{k}^{+}+2\zeta\nabla\zeta\varphi_{k}^{+}\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s=C_{R}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\left\lvert\zeta\nabla(\zeta\varphi_{k}^{+})+\zeta\nabla\zeta\varphi_{k}^{+}\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\leq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\lvert\nabla(\zeta\varphi_{k}^{+})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+C_{R}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\lvert\zeta\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+C_{R}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\lvert\nabla\zeta\rvert^{2}(\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s,

and, by using
A7
and
B7
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}(P(\varphi)(\sigma-\mu)-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)u)\,\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\left(\lVert P\rVert_{\infty}\lVert\sigma-\mu\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}+\lVert\mathbbm{h}\rVert_{\infty}\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\right)\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\zeta^{2}\varphi_{k}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\leq C_{R}\lVert\varphi_{k}^{+}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\lvert\zeta\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{R}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\lvert\zeta\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s.

Then, by putting all together and by taking the supremum over
(t_{0},t)
on the right-hand side, we obtain:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\sup_{s\in(t_{0},t)}\int_{\Omega}(\varphi_{k}^{+}\zeta)^{2}(s)\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\lvert\nabla(\zeta\varphi_{k}^{+})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}(\varphi_{k}^{+}\zeta)^{2}(t_{0})\,\mathrm{d}x+C_{R}\int_{Q_{t_{0},t}}\left(\lvert\nabla\zeta\rvert^{2}(\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}+(\varphi_{k}^{+})^{2}\zeta\zeta_{t}+\lvert\zeta\rvert^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(4.11)

Arguing in a similar fashion, one can easily see that inequality (
4.11
) also holds with
\varphi
replaced by
-\varphi
. Therefore, thanks to
[
31
, Chapter II, Remark 7.2]
, we can say that
\varphi
is an element of the class
\mathcal{B}(Q_{0,T},R,\gamma,r,0,\kappa)
in the sense of
[
31
, Chapter II, Section 7]
, for some
\gamma>0
,
r>2
and
\kappa>0
. Then, we can apply
[
31
, Chapter II, Theorem 7.1]
to infer the existence of a
\beta\in(0,1)
such that

\lvert\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,s)\rvert\leq C\left(\lvert x-y\rvert^{\beta}+\lvert t-s\rvert^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\right)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for any }(x,t),(y,s)\in\overline{\Omega}\times[0,T].

To get a similar estimate for
\sigma
, we argue in the same way by multiplying equation (
1.9
) by
\zeta^{2}\sigma_{k}^{+}
and integrating again on
Q_{t_{0},t}
. Here, the situation is easier since we just have the laplacian operator for
\sigma
and the reaction term and the source term can be treated essentially in the same way. Therefore, we can once again use
[
31
, Chapter II, Theorem 7.1 and Remark 7.2]
to conclude.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

The result of Proposition
4.5
implies in particular that
\varphi,\sigma\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega))
for some
\beta\in(0,1)
, uniformly with respect to the parameters, which means that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert(\varphi(t),\sigma(t))\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega)^{2}}\leq C.

This is what we will use in the following Theorem.

Now we are ready to prove the main results of this subsection:

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
\rho=1
,
s=2-\frac{2}{p}>1+\frac{N}{p}
, with
p\in(N+2,+\infty)
. Assume also hypotheses
A1
-
A7
and
B1
-
B7
.

Let
(\varphi,\sigma)
be the maximal solution to
(
1.7
)–(
1.11
)
in the sense of Definition
3.4
with
\chi=0
. Then the maximal solution is
global
, i.e. the maximal existence time
t^{+}=t^{+}(\varphi_{0},\sigma_{0},u,v)
is equal to
T
for any
(\varphi_{0},\sigma_{0})\in M^{2-2/p,p}
and for any fixed
u,v\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
.

Proof.

By Theorem
3.10
, let
(\varphi,\sigma)
be the maximal solution to
(
1.7
)–(
1.11
)
in the sense of Definition
3.4
with
\chi=0
on
I=[0,t^{+})
. Assume by contradiction that
t^{+}<T
, then since
(\varphi,\sigma)\in\mathcal{C}([0,t^{+});M^{2-2/p,p})
, by definition of maximal time-interval of existence, it should hold that
\lim_{t\to t^{+}}\lVert(\varphi(t),\sigma(t))\rVert_{W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega)}=+\infty
. However, by Lemma
3.11
and Proposition
4.5
, it follows that

\sup_{t\in[0,t^{+})}\lVert(\varphi(t),\sigma(t))\rVert_{W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(1+\sup_{t\in[0,t^{+}/2)}\lVert(\varphi(t),\sigma(t))\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}(\Omega)^{2}}\right)\leq C,

for
\beta\in(0,1)
, given by Proposition
4.5
.
We recall that Lemma
3.11
holds for
any

\beta\in(0,1)
, thus here we choose exactly the one provided by Proposition
4.5
.
Moreover, observe that we were allowed to apply Proposition
4.5
because
(\varphi_{0},\sigma_{0})\in W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\Omega)
. This contradicts the assumption, so the maximal solution can be continued to the whole interval
[0,T]
.
∎

In the end, we have shown the following result:

Theorem 4.8
.

Assume hypotheses
A1
–
A7
,
B1
–
B5
and
B7
.
Assume further that

\varphi_{0},\sigma_{0}\in H^{2}(\Omega)\text{ with }\begin{cases}\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\mu(0)=\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}(AF^{\prime}(\varphi_{0})+Ba\varphi_{0}-BJ\ast\varphi_{0})=0\\
\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\sigma_{0}=0\end{cases}\text{on $\partial\Omega$}.

(4.12)

Then, there exists a unique solution
(\varphi,\mu,\sigma)
to (
1.7
)–(
1.11
) such that

\varphi,\mu,\sigma\in W^{1,6}(0,T;L^{6}(\Omega))\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];W^{1,6}(\Omega))\cap L^{6}(0,T;W^{2,6}(\Omega))\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{Q_{T}}).

Moreover, there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the parameters of the system, such that the following estimate holds:

\lVert(\varphi,\mu,\sigma)\rVert_{(W^{1,6}(0,T;L^{6}(\Omega))\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];W^{1,6}(\Omega))\cap L^{6}(0,T;W^{2,6}(\Omega)))^{3}}\leq C.

(4.13)

Proof.

One just needs to apply Theorem
4.7
with
p=6
, by observing that
H^{2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega)
if
p\in(N+2,6]
and
N\leq 3
. Then, the same regularity for
\mu
easily follows by comparison in (
1.8
), since
F
is regular and
\varphi
is bounded. Note that the regularities
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];W^{1,6}(\Omega))
and
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{Q_{T}})
come from standard embeddings.
∎

Remark 4.9
.

Observe that on initial data
\varphi_{0}
and
\sigma_{0}
we now assume (
4.12
), as it is commonly done when seeking stronger solutions for Cahn-Hilliard type equations.

Remark 4.10
.

We also mention that the procedure used above also gives new regularity results for the standard non-local Cahn-Hilliard equation with constant mobility and regular potential, i.e. the system

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}-\Delta\mu=0\hskip 20.00003pt

in
Q_{T}
,

\displaystyle\mu=AF^{\prime}(\varphi)+Ba\varphi-BJ\ast\varphi\hskip 20.00003pt

in
Q_{T}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\mu=0\hskip 20.00003pt

on
\Sigma_{T}
,

\displaystyle\varphi(0)=\varphi_{0}\hskip 20.00003pt

in
\Omega
.

Indeed, without considering the equation for
\sigma
and by neglecting chemotaxis and forgetting the reaction and source terms, all the procedure in Sections
3
and
4
can be easily repeated. Then, under hypotheses
A2
–
A5
,
B1
–
B2
, if
\varphi_{0}\in W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega)
for
p\in(N+2,+\infty)
is such that
\partial_{n}\mu(0)=0
on
\partial\Omega
, one is able to prove that the unique solution to the non-local Cahn-Hilliard equation above is such that

\varphi,\mu\in W^{1,p}(0,T;L^{p}(\Omega))\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];W^{2-2/p,p}(\Omega))\cap L^{p}(0,T;W^{2,p}(\Omega))\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{Q_{T}}).

4.2
Continuous dependence on data

In this subsection, we prove that the strong solutions of Theorem
4.8
depend continuously on the controls
u
and
v
and initial data
\varphi_{0},\sigma_{0}
. This result will be crucial in proving differentiability properties of the control-to-state operator in the next section and will strongly use the regularity estimate (
4.13
).

Theorem 4.11
.

Assume hypotheses
A1
–
A7
,
B1
–
B4
and
B7
. Let
u_{1}
,
v_{1}
,
{\varphi_{0}}_{1}
,
{\sigma_{0}}_{1}
and
u_{2}
,
v_{2}
,
{\varphi_{0}}_{2}
,
{\sigma_{0}}_{2}
be two sets of data satisfying
B5
and
(
4.12
)
and let
(\varphi_{1},\mu_{1},\sigma_{1})
and
(\varphi_{2},\mu_{2},\sigma_{2})
two corresponding strong solutions as in Theorem
4.8
. Then, there exists a constant
K>0
, depending only on the data of the system and on the norms of
\{(u_{i},v_{i},{\varphi_{0}}_{i},{\sigma_{0}}_{i})\}_{i=1,2}
, but not on their difference, such that

\begin{split}&\lVert\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\rVert_{H^{1}(0,T;H)\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))}+\lVert\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{H^{1}(0,T;H)\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\cap L^{2}(0,T;W)}\\
&\qquad+\lVert\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{2}\rVert_{H^{1}(0,T;H)\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\cap L^{2}(0,T;W)}\\
&\quad\leq K\left(\lVert u_{1}-u_{2}\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert v_{1}-v_{2}\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert{\varphi_{0}}_{1}-{\varphi_{0}}_{2}\rVert_{V}+\lVert{\sigma_{0}}_{1}-{\sigma_{0}}_{2}\rVert_{V}\right).\end{split}

(4.14)

Proof.

Let
\varphi=\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}
,
\mu=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
,
\sigma=\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{2}
,
u=u_{1}-u_{2}
,
v=v_{1}-v_{2}
,
\varphi_{0}={\varphi_{0}}_{1}-{\varphi_{0}}_{2}
and
\sigma_{0}={\sigma_{0}}_{1}-{\sigma_{0}}_{2}
, then, up to adding and subtracting some terms, they solve:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\varphi

\displaystyle=\Delta\mu+P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu)+(P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})u-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2}))u_{2}

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(4.15)

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle=A(F^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}))+Ba\varphi-BJ\ast\varphi

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(4.16)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\sigma

\displaystyle=\Delta\sigma-P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu)-(P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2})+v

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(4.17)

paired with boundary and initial conditions:

\displaystyle\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\mu=\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\sigma=0\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{on }\Sigma_{T},

(4.18)

\displaystyle\varphi(0)=\varphi_{0},\hskip 10.00002pt\sigma(0)=\sigma_{0}\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }\Omega.

(4.19)

Now, for the first estimate, we test (
4.15
) by
\varphi
in
H
, (
4.17
) by
\sigma
in
H
and then sum them up to obtain:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}+(\nabla\mu,\nabla\varphi)_{H}+\lVert\nabla\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}=(P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu),\varphi-\sigma)_{H}

(4.20)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+((P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}),\varphi-\sigma)_{H}-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})u-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2}))u_{2},\varphi)_{H}+(v,\sigma)_{H}.

Next, we start by estimating the term
(\nabla\mu,\nabla\varphi)_{H}
. Indeed, by using equation (
4.16
), up to adding and subtracting some terms, we have to estimate:

\displaystyle(\nabla\mu,\nabla\varphi)_{H}

\displaystyle=((AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})+Ba)\nabla\varphi,\nabla\varphi)_{H}+A((F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\nabla\varphi_{2},\nabla\varphi)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+B(\nabla a\,\varphi,\nabla\varphi)_{H}-B(\nabla J\ast\varphi,\nabla\varphi)_{H}.

Hence, by using hypotheses
A3
,
A2
, Hölder, Gagliardo-Nirenberg (
2.1
) and Young’s inequalities, together with the fact that
F^{\prime\prime}
is locally Lipschitz and
\varphi_{i}
,
i=1,2
, is globally bounded, we infer that

\displaystyle(\nabla\mu,\nabla\varphi)_{H}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}-A\lVert F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{3}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{H}-2Bb^{\ast}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{3}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{H}-2Bb^{\ast}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{3/2}_{H}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{1/2}_{H}-2Bb^{\ast}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\left(1+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}.

(4.21)

Then, regarding the terms on the right-hand side of (
4.20
), we use again (
4.16
), the local Lipschitz continuity of
F^{\prime}
,
P
and
\mathbbm{h}
, Hölder and Young’s inequalities, hypothesis
A2
, the embedding
V\hookrightarrow L^{6}(\Omega)
and Remark
4.3
, to deduce that

\displaystyle(P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu),\varphi-\sigma)_{H}\leq\lVert P(\varphi_{1})\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lVert\sigma-\mu\rVert_{H}\lVert\varphi-\sigma\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=C\lVert\sigma-A(F^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}))-Ba\varphi+BJ\ast\varphi\rVert_{H}(\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}+\lVert\sigma\rVert_{H})

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C(\lVert\sigma\rVert_{H}+C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}+2Ba^{*}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H})(\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}+\lVert\sigma\rVert_{H})

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H},

\displaystyle((P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}),\varphi-\sigma)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lVert P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{H}\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi-\sigma\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{V}(\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V}+\lVert\sigma\rVert_{V})

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\lVert\nabla\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}+C(1+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V})\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H},

\displaystyle(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})u-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2}))u_{2},\varphi)_{H}\leq C\lVert u\rVert_{H}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}+\lVert u_{2}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\lVert\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{H}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\lVert u\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H},

\displaystyle(v,\sigma)_{H}\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert v\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}.

Then, by putting all together and integrating on
(0,t)
, for any
t\in(0,T)
, from (
4.20
) we arrive at the estimate:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lVert\varphi(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\lVert\sigma(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\lVert\sigma_{0}\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\int_{0}^{T}(1+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V})\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert u\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert v\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
, since
\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];W^{1,6}(\Omega))
by Theorem
4.8
, and also
\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
, again by Theorem
4.8
, given that
V\hookrightarrow W^{1,6}(\Omega)
. Therefore, we can apply Gronwall’s inequality to deduce the following continuous dependence estimate:

\begin{split}&\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)}+\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}+\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}\\
&\quad\leq C\left(\lVert u_{1}-u_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert v_{1}-v_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert{\varphi_{0}}_{1}-{\varphi_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert{\sigma_{0}}_{1}-{\sigma_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{H}\right),\end{split}

(4.22)

where
C>0
depends only on the parameters of the system and on the norms of the data
\{(u_{i},v_{i},{\varphi_{0}}_{i},{\sigma_{0}}_{i})\}_{i=1,2}
, but not on their difference. Now we argue essentially by comparison to get the same estimates also on
\mu
. Indeed, by estimating the
H
-norm of
\mu
through (
1.8
) and by using hypothesis
A2
and the local Lipschitz continuity of
F^{\prime}
, we get that

\displaystyle\lVert\mu\rVert_{H}\leq A\lVert F^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime}(\varphi_{2})\rVert^{2}_{H}+2Ba^{*}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}.

In the same way, we can also compute the
H
-norm of
\nabla\mu
through (
4.16
) and, by exploiting hypothesis
A2
, Hölder’s inequality, the embedding
V\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\Omega)
, the local Lipschitz continuity of
F^{\prime\prime}
and Remark
4.3
, we see that

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\mu\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\leq A\lVert F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})\nabla\varphi\rVert_{H}+A\lVert(F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{H}+B\lVert a\nabla\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+B\lVert\nabla a\varphi\rVert_{H}+B\lVert\nabla J\ast\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\leq A\lVert F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{H}+A\lVert F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+Bb^{\ast}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{H}+2Bb^{\ast}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V}+C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)})\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V}\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V},

where
\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
, since
\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];W^{1,6}(\Omega))
by Theorem
4.8
and
L^{6}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\Omega)
. Then, directly from (
4.22
), we also infer that

\begin{split}&\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}\leq\\
&\quad\leq C\left(\lVert u_{1}-u_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert v_{1}-v_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert{\varphi_{0}}_{1}-{\varphi_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert{\sigma_{0}}_{1}-{\sigma_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{H}\right).\end{split}

(4.23)

For the second estimate, we now test (
4.17
) in
H
by
\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma
and we get:

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\nabla\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert\Delta\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}=-(P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu),\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-((P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}),\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma)_{H}+(u,\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma)_{H}.

We can now easily estimate the terms on the right-hand side by using similar techniques to the ones used before, indeed we have that

\displaystyle(P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu),\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma)_{H}\leq\lVert P(\varphi_{1})\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lVert\sigma-\mu\rVert_{H}\lVert\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{1}{4}\lVert\sigma_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{4}\lVert\Delta\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{H},

\displaystyle((P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}),\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lVert P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{1}{4}\lVert\sigma_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{4}\lVert\Delta\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V},

\displaystyle(v,\sigma_{t}-\Delta\sigma)_{H}\leq\frac{1}{4}\lVert\sigma_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{4}\lVert\Delta\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert v\rVert^{2}_{H}.

Then, by also integrating on
(0,t)
, for any
t\in(0,T)
, we infer that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\sigma_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\lVert\nabla\sigma(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\Delta\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lVert\sigma_{0}\rVert^{2}_{V}+C\int_{0}^{T}(1+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V})\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert v\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Hence, since
\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
by Theorem
4.8
, we can use (
4.22
) and apply Gronwall’s inequality to conclude that

\begin{split}&\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;H)\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\cap L^{2}(0,T;W)}\\
&\quad\leq C\left(\lVert u_{1}-u_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert v_{1}-v_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert{\varphi_{0}}_{1}-{\varphi_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert{\sigma_{0}}_{1}-{\sigma_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}\right).\end{split}

(4.24)

Next, we want to prove similar continuous dependence estimates also on
\varphi
and
\mu
. To do this, we test equation (
4.15
) by
\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t}
and we obtain that

\begin{split}&(\varphi_{t},\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\nabla\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}-B(\nabla\mu,\nabla J\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}\\
&\quad=(P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu),\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}+((P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}),\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}\\
&\qquad-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})u-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2}))u_{2},\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t})_{H},\end{split}

(4.25)

where we observe that, by taking the time derivative of (
4.16
) and adjusting some terms, we have that

\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t}=AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})\varphi_{t}+Ba\varphi_{t}+A(F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})){\varphi_{2}}_{t}.

(4.26)

The reason why we test by
\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t}
is twofold. Firstly, we want to be able to get a positive term on
\varphi_{t}
out of
(\varphi_{t},\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}
and, secondly, we also want to control the extra term
-B(\nabla\mu,\nabla J\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}
. Indeed, by using hypothesis
A3
, Hölder and Young’s inequalities, the local Lipschitz continuity of
F^{\prime\prime}
and Remark
4.3
, we infer that

\displaystyle(\varphi_{t},\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}

\displaystyle=((AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})+Ba)\varphi_{t},\varphi_{t})_{H}+A((F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})){\varphi_{2}}_{t},\varphi_{t})_{H}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{3}(\Omega)}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3c_{0}}{4}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V},

where we recall that
\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\in L^{3}(0,T)
because
{\varphi_{2}}_{t}
is bounded in
L^{6}(0,T;L^{6}(\Omega))
by Theorem
4.8
. Moreover, we can also estimate by standard means the other term:

B(\nabla\mu,\nabla J\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}\leq Bb^{\ast}\lVert\nabla\mu\rVert_{H}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert_{H}\leq\frac{c_{0}}{8}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{V}.

Next, we estimate the three terms on the right-hand side of (
4.25
) by using again (
4.26
). Indeed, by using a combination of Hölder and Young’s inequalities, Sobolev embeddings, the local Lipschitz continuity of
F^{\prime\prime}
,
P
and
\mathbbm{h}
and Remark
4.3
, we obtain that

\displaystyle(P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu),\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=(P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu),(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})+Ba)\varphi_{t}+A(F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})){\varphi_{2}}_{t})_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\lVert\sigma-\mu\rVert_{H}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert_{H}+C\lVert\sigma-\mu\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\lVert\sigma-\mu\rVert_{H}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert_{H}+C\lVert\sigma-\mu\rVert_{V}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{c_{0}}{8}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+C(1+\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H})\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{V},

\displaystyle((P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}),\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=((P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}),(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})+Ba)\varphi_{t}+A(F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})){\varphi_{2}}_{t})_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\lVert P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\lVert P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V}\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{V}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert_{H}+C\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{V}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{c_{0}}{8}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\left(\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{V}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}\right)\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V},

\displaystyle(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})u-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2}))u_{2},\mu_{t}-BJ\ast\varphi_{t})_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})u-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2}))u_{2},(AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})+Ba)\varphi_{t}+A(F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})){\varphi_{2}}_{t})_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\lVert u\rVert_{H}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert_{H}+C\lVert u\rVert_{H}\lVert F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{3}(\Omega)}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\lVert\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{H}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert_{H}+\lVert\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2})\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{c_{0}}{8}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert u\rVert^{2}_{H}+\left(1+\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}^{2}+\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}\right)\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V}.

Then, by collecting all the estimates, starting from (
4.25
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{4}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\nabla\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}\leq C\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{V}+C\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{V}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C\left(1+\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{V}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}+\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}\right)\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V},

where we observe that
\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\in L^{3}(0,T)
,
\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
and
\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}\in L^{6}(0,T)
by Theorem
4.8
. Moreover, we can see that, by rewriting the final line of (
4.2
) and using Cauchy-Schwarz and Young’s inequalities on the left-hand side, we have the estimate

\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}\leq C\lVert\nabla\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\left(1+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H},

which then, since
\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T)
, implies that

\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V}\leq C\lVert\nabla\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}.

(4.27)

Therefore, by using (
4.27
) and integrating on
(0,t)
, for any
t\in(0,T)
, the previous inequality now becomes

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\lVert\nabla\mu(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}\leq\lVert\nabla\mu(0)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{V}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{V}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C\int_{0}^{T}\left(1+\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{V}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}+\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}\right)\lVert\nabla\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s
