\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C\int_{0}^{T}\left(1+\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert_{V}\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}+\lVert{\varphi_{2}}_{t}\rVert_{H}\right)\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s,

where the expression between the parentheses is integrable in time, due to the previous remarks. Moreover, since
{\varphi_{0}}_{i}\in H^{2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\Omega)
,
i=1,2
, we can also estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\mu(0)\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle=A\lVert F^{\prime\prime}({\varphi_{0}}_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}({\varphi_{0}}_{2})\nabla{\varphi_{0}}_{2}\rVert_{H}+A\lVert F^{\prime\prime}({\varphi_{0}}_{1})\nabla\varphi_{0}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+B\lVert a\nabla\varphi_{0}\rVert_{H}+B\lVert\nabla a\varphi_{0}\rVert_{H}+B\lVert\nabla J\ast\varphi_{0}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert{\varphi_{0}}_{1}\rVert_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}+C\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert_{V}\leq C\lVert\varphi_{0}\rVert_{V}.

Then, by using Gronwall’s inequality, together with (
4.22
) and (
4.23
), we infer that

\begin{split}&\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;H)}+\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;V)}\\
&\quad\leq C\left(\lVert u_{1}-u_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert v_{1}-v_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert{\varphi_{0}}_{1}-{\varphi_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert{\sigma_{0}}_{1}-{\sigma_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}\right).\end{split}

(4.28)

Moreover, by comparison with (
4.26
) and (
4.27
), one can also easily see that

\begin{split}&\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;H)}+\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;V)}\\
&\quad\leq C\left(\lVert u_{1}-u_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert v_{1}-v_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert{\varphi_{0}}_{1}-{\varphi_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert{\sigma_{0}}_{1}-{\sigma_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}\right).\end{split}

(4.29)

Next, we test (
4.15
) by
-\Delta\mu
in
H
and we get:

\displaystyle\lVert\Delta\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle=(\varphi_{t},\Delta\mu)_{H}-(P(\varphi_{1})(\sigma-\mu),\Delta\mu)_{H}-((P(\varphi_{1})-P(\varphi_{2}))(\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}),\Delta\mu)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})u-(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{1})-\mathbbm{h}(\varphi_{2}))u_{2},\Delta\mu)_{H}.

Then, by using Cauchy-Schwarz and Young’s inequality, together with the local Lipschitz continuity of
P
and
\mathbbm{h}
and Remark
4.3
, we infer that

\displaystyle\lVert\Delta\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert\Delta\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\lVert\varphi_{t}\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\sigma\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C\left(1+\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert u\rVert^{2}_{H}.

Hence, we can integrate on
(0,T)
and use (
4.22
), (
4.23
) and (
4.28
), together with the fact that
\lVert\sigma_{2}-\mu_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
by Theorem
4.8
, to deduce that

\begin{split}&\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;W)}\\
&\quad\leq C\left(\lVert u_{1}-u_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert v_{1}-v_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert{\varphi_{0}}_{1}-{\varphi_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert{\sigma_{0}}_{1}-{\sigma_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}\right).\end{split}

(4.30)

Finally, for any
i,j=1,2,3
, we apply the differential operator
\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}
to (
4.16
), which makes sense in
H
, and we test the resulting equation by
\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi
. Then, after careful rewriting of the terms arising from the derivatives of
F
, up to adding and subtracting some of them, we get:

\displaystyle(\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\mu,\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi)_{H}=((AF^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})+Ba)\,\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi,\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi)_{H}+((F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi_{2},\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+(F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})(\partial_{x_{i}}\varphi_{1}+\partial_{x_{i}}\varphi_{2})\,\partial_{x_{j}}\varphi,\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi)_{H}+((F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})-F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\partial_{x_{i}}\varphi_{2}\,\partial_{x_{j}}\varphi_{2},\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+(B(\partial_{x_{i}}a\,\partial_{x_{j}}\varphi+\partial_{x_{j}}a\,\partial_{x_{i}}\varphi),\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi)_{H}+(B\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}a\,\varphi,\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi)_{H}-B(\partial_{x_{i}}(\partial_{x_{j}}J\ast\varphi),\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi)_{H}.

Hence, by using hypotheses
A3
,
B1
, Remark
3.7
, Remark
4.3
, the local Lipschitz continuity of
F^{\prime\prime}
and
F^{\prime\prime\prime}
, Sobolev embeddings and Hölder, Young, Gagliardo-Nirenberg and Agmon’s inequalities (see
2.1
and
2.2
), we can estimate:

\displaystyle c_{0}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}\leq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\mu\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi_{2}\rVert_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\lVert\nabla\varphi_{1}+\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert_{L^{3}(\Omega)}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert_{H}+C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{6}\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{2}_{6}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}a\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert_{H}+C\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{W}+C\lVert\varphi_{2}\rVert_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V}^{1/2}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{3/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\lVert\nabla\varphi_{1}+\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi\rVert^{1/2}_{H}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{3/2}_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V}\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{V}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert_{H}+C\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\lVert\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+C\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{W}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\left(1+\lVert\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{1}+\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V},

where
\lVert\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{1}+\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\in L^{3/2}(0,T)
, since, by Theorem
4.8
,
\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];W^{1,6}(\Omega))\cap L^{6}(0,T;W^{2,6}(\Omega))
, and
H^{2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{1,6}(\Omega)
. Therefore, by summing on
i,j=1,2,3
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{2}\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{W}+C\left(1+\lVert\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{1}+\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\nabla\varphi_{2}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{V},

starting from which, by integrating on
(0,T)
and using the previous estimates (
4.29
) and (
4.30
), we conclude that

\begin{split}&\lVert\varphi\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))}\\
&\leq C\left(\lVert u_{1}-u_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert v_{1}-v_{2}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert{\varphi_{0}}_{1}-{\varphi_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert{\sigma_{0}}_{1}-{\sigma_{0}}_{2}\rVert^{2}_{V}\right).\end{split}

(4.31)

This concludes the proof of Theorem
4.11
.
∎

5
Optimal control problem

From now on, we consider the initial data
\varphi_{0}
and
\sigma_{0}
, satisfying (
4.12
), fixed. As an application of the strong well-posedness that we were able to prove in Theorems
4.8
and
4.11
, we consider the control problem (CP), which we recall below:

(CP)
Minimise the cost functional

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}(\varphi,\sigma,u,v)&=\,\frac{\alpha_{\Omega}}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\varphi(T)-\varphi_{\Omega}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{\alpha_{Q}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\varphi-\varphi_{Q}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\\
&\quad+\frac{\beta_{\Omega}}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\sigma(T)-\sigma_{\Omega}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{\beta_{Q}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\sigma-\sigma_{Q}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\\
&\quad+\frac{\alpha_{u}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|u|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\frac{\beta_{v}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,\end{split}

subject to the control constraints

\begin{split}&u\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}:=\{u\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})\mid u_{\text{min}}\leq u\leq u_{\text{max}}\text{ a.e.\penalty\ in }Q_{T}\},\\
&v\in\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}:=\{v\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})\mid v_{\text{min}}\leq v\leq v_{\text{max}}\text{ a.e.\penalty\ in }Q_{T}\},\end{split}

and to the state system (
1.7
)-(
1.11
)
.

Regarding the parameters at play, we make the following hypotheses:

C1.

\alpha_{\Omega},\alpha_{Q},\beta_{\Omega},\beta_{Q},\beta_{u}\geq 0
, but not all equal to
0
.

C2.

\varphi_{\Omega},\sigma_{\Omega}\in L^{2}(\Omega)
and
\varphi_{Q},\sigma_{Q}\in L^{2}(Q_{T})
.

C3.

u_{\text{min}},u_{\text{max}},v_{\text{min}},v_{\text{max}}\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
, with
u_{\text{min}}\leq u_{\text{max}}
and
v_{\text{min}}\leq v_{\text{max}}
a.e. in
\Omega
.

C4.

P,\mathbbm{h}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.

C5.

\varphi_{0},\sigma_{0}\in H^{2}(\Omega)
with
\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}(AF^{\prime}(\varphi_{0})+Ba\varphi_{0}-BJ\ast\varphi_{0})=\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\sigma_{0}=0
on
\partial\Omega
.

Remark 5.1
.

For modelling reasons, in practice one generally takes
\mathbbm{h}
to be non-negative and
u_{\text{min}}\geq 0
, since the radiotherapy
u
should only act to decrease the tumour proliferation.

By Theorem
4.8
, we know that for any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
there exists a unique strong solution
(\varphi,\mu,\sigma)\in\mathbb{X}
to (
1.7
)–(
1.11
), where

\displaystyle\mathbb{X}

\displaystyle:=\left(W^{1,6}(0,T;L^{6}(\Omega))\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];W^{1,6}(\Omega))\cap L^{6}(0,T;W^{2,6}(\Omega))\right)^{3},

therefore the optimal control problem (CP) is well-defined. Our goal is to prove existence of an optimal control and then find the first-order necessary optimality conditions.
We stress that to prove such optimality conditions, we need to study the differentiability of the control-to-state operator.
Hence, a strong continuous dependence estimate like (
4.14
) is necessary and we recall that, to prove it, we heavily relied on the global maximal regularity results.
We first begin with the following existence result for optimal controls.

Theorem 5.2
.

Assume hypotheses
A1
–
A7
,
B1
–
B2
and
C1
–
C5
. Then the optimal control problem (CP) admits at least one solution
(\overline{u},\overline{v})\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
, such that if
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})
is the solution to (
1.7
)–(
1.11
) associated to
(\overline{u},\overline{v})
, one has that

\mathcal{J}(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\sigma},\overline{u},\overline{v})=\min_{(u,v)\,\in\,\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}}\,\mathcal{J}(\varphi,\sigma,u,v).

(5.1)

Proof.

The argument is standard and relies on the direct method of Calculus of Variations, therefore we omit it for the sake of brevity. For more details on the procedure, we refer the interested reader to
[
17
, Theorem 4.2]
.
∎

5.1
Linearised system

We now want to study Fréchet-differentiability properties of the control-to-state operator, which maps any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
into the corresponding solution of the state system. The first step consists in deriving the linearised version of system (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) and in proving its well-posedness, since it is generally the
ansatz
for the expression of the Fréchet derivative. Indeed, we fix a state
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})\in\mathbb{X}
corresponding to
(\overline{u},\overline{v})\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
and linearise near
(\overline{u},\overline{v})
:

\varphi=\overline{\varphi}+\xi,\,\mu=\overline{\mu}+\eta,\,\sigma=\overline{\sigma}+\rho,\,u=\overline{u}+h,v=\overline{v}+k,

with
(h,k)\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2}
. Then, by approximating the non-linearities at the first order of their Taylor expansion, we see that
(\xi,\eta,\rho)
satisfy the equations:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\xi-\Delta\eta=P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi+P(\overline{\varphi})(\rho-\eta)-\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}\,\xi-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})h

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(5.2)

\displaystyle\eta=AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\xi+Ba\xi-BJ\ast\xi

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(5.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho-\Delta\rho=-P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi-P(\overline{\varphi})(\rho-\eta)+k

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(5.4)

together with boundary and initial conditions:

\displaystyle\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\eta=\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\rho=0\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{on }\Sigma_{T},

(5.5)

\displaystyle\xi(0)=0,\hskip 10.00002pt\rho(0)=0\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }\Omega.

(5.6)

Theorem 5.3
.

Assume hypotheses
A1
–
A7
,
B1
–
B2
and
C4
–
C5
. Let
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})\in\mathbb{X}
be the strong solution to (
1.7
)–(
1.11
), corresponding to
(\overline{u},\overline{v})\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
. Then, for any
(h,k)\in L^{2}(0,T;H)\times L^{2}(0,T;H)
, the linearised system (
5.2
)–(
5.6
) admits a unique weak solution, which is uniformly bounded in the following spaces

\displaystyle\xi\in H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V),

\displaystyle\eta\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V),

\displaystyle\rho\in H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V),

and fulfils (
5.2
)–(
5.6
) in variational form, i.e. it satisfies

\displaystyle\langle\xi_{t},w\rangle_{V}+(\nabla\eta,\nabla w)_{H}=(P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi+P(\overline{\varphi})(\rho-\eta)-\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}\,\xi-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})h,w)_{H},

\displaystyle(\eta,w)_{H}=(AF^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\xi+Ba\xi-BJ\ast\xi,w)_{H},

\displaystyle\langle\rho_{t},w\rangle_{V}+(\nabla\rho,\nabla w)_{H}=-(P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi+P(\overline{\varphi})(\rho-\eta),w)_{H}+(k,w)_{H},

for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
and for any
w\in V
, and
\xi(0)=0
,
\rho(0)=0
.

Proof.

We proceed formally, but we recall that the argument can be made rigorous by employing a Faedo-Galerkin discretisation scheme, with discrete spaces made of eigenvectors of the operator
\mathcal{N}
. Then, being the system linear, it is a standard matter to pass to the limit in the discretisation framework and recover a weak solution with the expected regularities. For the main estimate, we test equation (
5.2
) by
\xi
, (
5.4
) by
\rho
and sum them up, to obtain:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}+(\nabla\eta,\nabla\xi)_{H}+\lVert\nabla\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq(P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi,\xi-\rho)_{H}+(P(\overline{\varphi})(\rho-\eta),\xi-\rho)_{H}-(\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}\,\xi+\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})h,\xi)_{H}+(k,\rho)_{H}.

Then, by using Remark
4.3
, Cauchy-Schwarz and Young’s inequalities and by recalling that
\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
by Theorem
4.8
, we estimate the right-hand side as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}+(\nabla\eta,\nabla\xi)_{H}+\lVert\nabla\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\left(1+\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert k\rVert^{2}_{H}.

Next, to close the estimate, we further test (
5.3
) by
-\Delta\xi
, which is possible within the discretisation framework, and integrate by parts, by recalling that, due to the fact that the discrete spaces are made of functions satisfying homogeneous Neumann boundary conditions, no extra boundary terms appear. Then, by exploiting Hölder, Young and Gagliardo-Nirenberg (
2.1
) inequalities, together with Remark
4.3
and hypotheses
A3
and
A2
, we infer that

\displaystyle(\nabla\eta,\nabla\xi)_{H}

\displaystyle=(AF^{\prime\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\nabla\overline{\varphi}\,\xi,\nabla\xi)_{H}+((AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})+Ba)\nabla\xi,\nabla\xi)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+(B\nabla a\,\xi,\nabla\xi)_{H}-(B\nabla J\ast\xi,\nabla\xi)_{H}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\xi\rVert_{L^{3}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert_{H}-2Bb^{\ast}\lVert\xi\rVert_{H}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{1/2}_{H}\lVert\xi\rVert^{3/2}_{V}-2Bb^{\ast}\lVert\xi\rVert_{H}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{0}}{2}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\left(1+\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H},

where
\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
by Theorem
4.8
and Sobolev embeddings. Therefore, by putting all together, we have the estimate:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert\nabla\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\left(1+\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert k\rVert^{2}_{H}.

Now observe that, by comparison in equation (
5.3
), thanks to Remark
4.3
and hypothesis
A2
, one can easily see that

\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{H}\leq C\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}.

(5.7)

Hence, by also integrating on
(0,t)
, for any
t\in(0,T)
, from the previous inequality we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lVert\xi(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\lVert\rho(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\left(1+\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\rho\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert k\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s,

which, by Gronwall’s lemma, implies the following uniform estimate:

\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}+\lVert\rho\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}\leq C\left(\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert k\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}\right),

(5.8)

with
C>0
depending only on the parameters of the system.
Next, by testing (
5.3
) by
-\Delta\eta
, integrating by parts and using Hölder and Young’s inequalities, we obtain that

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\eta\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle=(AF^{\prime\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\nabla\overline{\varphi}\,\xi,\nabla\eta)_{H}+((AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})+Ba)\nabla\xi,\nabla\eta)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+(B\nabla a\,\xi,\nabla\eta)_{H}-(B\nabla J\ast\xi,\nabla\eta)_{H}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert\nabla\eta\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\underbrace{\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{L^{4}(\Omega)}}_{\in L^{\infty}(0,T)}\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{L^{4}(\Omega)}+C\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{V}\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert\nabla\eta\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{V},

which, by integrating on
(0,T)
and applying (
5.8
), together with (
5.7
), implies that

\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}\leq C\left(\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert k\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}\right).

Finally, by comparison in (
5.2
) and (
5.4
), it also follows that

\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})}+\lVert\rho\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})}\leq C\left(\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert k\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}\right).

In the end, by also using standard embeddings of Lebesgue-Bochner spaces on a Hilbert triplet, we have shown that there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the parameters of the system, such that

\begin{split}&\lVert\xi\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}+\lVert\eta\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}\\
&\quad+\lVert\rho\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}\leq C\left(\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}+\lVert k\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H)}\right).\end{split}

(5.9)

With this estimate, it is a standard matter to pass to the limit in the discretisation and show the existence of a weak solution to (
5.2
)–(
5.6
). Moreover, due to the linearity of the system, this same estimate also gives uniqueness of the solution. This concludes the proof of Theorem
5.3
.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

We observe that the linearised system (
5.2
)–(
5.6
) is exactly the same as the abstract linearised system (
3.2
) with
\boldsymbol{\uppsi}=(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\sigma})^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{\upxi}=(\xi,\rho)^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{f}=(-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})h,k)^{\top}
and
\boldsymbol{g}=\boldsymbol{0}
. This means that, since
(h,k)
can be taken in
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2}
and
\overline{\varphi}
can be embedded into
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{Q_{T}})
, one could follow the same argument used in the first part of the proof of Theorem
3.10
, to say that there exists a unique maximal solution with regularity

\xi,\rho\in W^{1,p}(0,T;L^{p}(\Omega))\cap L^{p}(0,T;W^{2,p}(\Omega)),\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for any $p>N+2$.}

This can clearly be done in place of the previous proof, but the actual regularity that we need on the linearised system to study the optimal control problem is way less that the one guaranteed by maximal regularity theory. This is the reason we also provided the proof above.

5.2
Differentiability of the control-to-state operator

In order to study the
control-to-state operator

\mathcal{S}
, which associates to any control
(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
the corresponding solution of the system (
1.7
)–(
1.11
), we introduce the following spaces:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Y}

\displaystyle:=(H^{1}(0,T;H)\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega)))^{3},

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}

\displaystyle:=(H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V))\times(L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V))

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\times(H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)).

Observe that the space of strong solutions
\mathbb{X}
is continuously embedded into
\mathbb{Y}
, which is exactly the space where we proved the continuous dependence estimates. Indeed, from Theorem
4.8
and Theorem
4.11
we respectively know that

\mathcal{S}:L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2}\to\mathbb{X}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{is well-defined and}

\mathcal{S}:L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2}\to\mathbb{Y}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{is locally Lipschitz-continuous.}

Now, for
R>0
, we fix an open set
\mathcal{U}_{R}\times\mathcal{V}_{R}\subseteq L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{R}\times\mathcal{V}_{R}
. Indeed, by hypothesis
C3
, we can take:

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{R}:=\{u\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})\mid\lVert u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}<M_{u}+R\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{R}:=\{v\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})\mid\lVert v\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}<M_{v}+R\},

where
M_{u}=\lVert u_{\text{max}}\rVert_{\infty}
and
M_{v}=\lVert v_{\text{max}}\rVert_{\infty}
.
Note that, in
\mathcal{U}_{R}\times\mathcal{V}_{R}
, the continuous dependence estimate of Theorem
4.11
holds with
K
depending only on
R
and the fixed data of the system.
Our aim is to show that
\mathcal{S}:\mathcal{U}_{R}\times\mathcal{V}_{R}\to\mathbb{W}
is also Fréchet-differentiable in the larger space
\mathbb{W}
.
Indeed, we can prove the following theorem:

Theorem 5.5
.

Assume hypothesis
A1
–
A7
,
B1
–
B2
and
C4
–
C5
. Then
\mathcal{S}:\mathcal{U}_{R}\times\mathcal{V}_{R}\to\mathbb{W}
is Fréchet-differentiable, i.e. for any
(\overline{u},\overline{v})\in\mathcal{U}_{R}\times\mathcal{V}_{R}
there exists a unique Fréchet-derivative
D\mathcal{S}(\overline{u},\overline{v})\in\mathcal{L}(L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2},\mathbb{W})
such that:

\frac{\lVert\mathcal{S}(\overline{u}+h,\overline{v}+k)-\mathcal{S}(\overline{u},\overline{v})-D\mathcal{S}(\overline{u},\overline{v})[h,k]\rVert_{\mathbb{W}}}{\lVert(h,k)\rVert_{L^{2}(Q_{T})^{2}}}\to 0\hskip 10.00002pt\text{as }\lVert(h,k)\rVert_{L^{2}(Q_{T})^{2}}\to 0.

(5.10)

Moreover, for any
(h,k)\in L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2}
, the Fréchet-derivative at
(\overline{u},\overline{v})
in
(h,k)
, which we denote by
D\mathcal{S}(\overline{u},\overline{v})[h,k]
, is defined as the solution
(\xi,\eta,\rho)
to the linearised system (
5.2
)–(
5.6
) corresponding to
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})=\mathcal{S}(\overline{u},\overline{v})
, with data
h
and
k
.

Remark 5.6
.

Note that, by Theorem
5.3
,
D\mathcal{S}(\overline{u},\overline{v})
as defined above actually belongs to the space of continuous linear operators
\mathcal{L}(L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2},\mathbb{W})
. Observe also that (
5.10
) shows Fréchet-differentiability with respect to the
L^{2}(Q_{T})
norm, but clearly, since
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(Q_{T})
, this also implies Fréchet-differentiability in the correct space.

Proof.

We observe that it is sufficient to prove the result for any small enough perturbation
(h,k)
, i.e. we fix
\Lambda>0
and consider only perturbations such that

\lVert(h,k)\rVert_{L^{2}(Q_{T})^{2}}\leq\Lambda.

(5.11)

Now, we fix
\overline{u}
,
\overline{v}
,
h
and
k
as above and consider

\displaystyle(\varphi,\mu,\sigma):=\mathcal{S}(\overline{u}+h,\overline{v}+k),

\displaystyle(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma}):=\mathcal{S}(\overline{u},\overline{v}),

\displaystyle(\xi,\eta,\rho)\text{ as the solution to \eqref{eq:xi}--\eqref{icl} with respect to }(h,k).

In order to show Fréchet-differentiability, then, it is enough to show that there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the parameters of the system and possibly on
\Lambda
, and an exponent
s>2
such that

\lVert(\varphi,\mu,\sigma)-(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})-(\xi,\eta,\rho)\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{W}}\leq C\lVert(h,k)\rVert^{s}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})^{2}}.

To do this, we introduce the additional variables

\displaystyle\psi:=\varphi-\overline{\varphi}-\xi\in H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V),

\displaystyle\zeta:=\mu-\overline{\mu}-\eta\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V),

\displaystyle\theta:=\sigma-\overline{\sigma}-\rho\in H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V),

which by Theorems
4.8
and
5.3
enjoy the regularities shown above. Then, this is equivalent to showing that

\lVert(\psi,\zeta,\theta)\rVert_{\mathbb{W}}^{2}\leq C\lVert(h,k)\rVert^{s}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})^{2}}.

(5.12)

Moreover, by inserting the equations solved by the variables in the definitions of
\psi
,
\zeta
and
\theta
and exploiting the linearity of the involved differential operators, we infer that these new variables formally satisfy the equations:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\psi-\Delta\zeta=Q^{h}-U^{h}\,\,\,

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(5.13)

\displaystyle\zeta=AF^{h}+Ba\psi-BJ\ast\psi\hskip 10.00002pt

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(5.14)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta-\Delta\theta=-Q^{h}\hskip 10.00002pt

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(5.15)

together with boundary and initial conditions:

\displaystyle\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\zeta=\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}\theta=0\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{on }\Sigma_{T},

(5.16)

\displaystyle\psi(0)=0,\hskip 10.00002pt\theta(0)=0\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }\Omega,

(5.17)

where:

\displaystyle F^{h}

\displaystyle=F^{\prime}(\varphi)-F^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})-F^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\xi,

\displaystyle Q^{h}

\displaystyle=P(\varphi)(\sigma-\mu)-P(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})-P(\overline{\varphi})(\rho-\eta)-P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi,

\displaystyle U^{h}

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{h}(\overline{u}+h)-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}\xi.

Note that, to be precise, system (
5.13
)–(
5.17
) has to be understood in weak sense, i.e. through a variational formulation, since only weak regularity is available for the linearised variables
(\xi,\eta,\rho)
.
Before going on, we can rewrite in a better way the terms
F^{h}
and
Q^{h}
, by using the following version of Taylor’s theorem with integral remainder for any real function
f\in\mathcal{C}^{2}
at a point
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
:

f(x)=f(x_{0})+f^{\prime}(x_{0})(x-x_{0})+\left(\int_{0}^{1}(1-z)f^{\prime\prime}(x_{0}+z(x-x_{0}))\,\mathrm{d}z\right)(x-x_{0})^{2}.

Indeed, with straightforward calculations one can see that

\displaystyle F^{h}=F^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\psi+R_{1}^{h}(\varphi-\overline{\varphi})^{2},

\displaystyle U^{h}=\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\psi\overline{u}+(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi}))h+R_{2}^{h}(\varphi-\overline{\varphi})^{2}\overline{u},

and also, up to adding and subtracting some additional terms, that

\displaystyle Q^{h}=P(\overline{\varphi})(\theta-\zeta)+P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\,\psi

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+(P(\varphi)-P(\overline{\varphi}))[(\sigma-\overline{\sigma})-(\mu-\overline{\mu})]+R_{3}^{h}(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})(\varphi-\overline{\varphi})^{2},

where

\displaystyle R_{1}^{h}=\int_{0}^{1}(1-z)F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi}+z(\varphi-\overline{\varphi}))\,\mathrm{d}z,\hskip 10.00002ptR_{2}^{h}=\int_{0}^{1}(1-z)\mathbbm{h}^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi}+z(\varphi-\overline{\varphi}))\,\mathrm{d}z,

\displaystyle R_{3}^{h}=\int_{0}^{1}(1-z)P^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi}+z(\varphi-\overline{\varphi}))\,\mathrm{d}z.

We observe that, exactly as in
[
17
, Proof of Theorem 4.4]
, by exploiting the strong regularity of
\varphi
and
\overline{\varphi}
given by Theorem
4.8
, together with hypotheses
B2
and
C4
, we can show that there exists a constant
C_{\Lambda}>0
, depending only on the parameters of the system and possibly on
\Lambda
, such that

\lVert R_{1}^{h}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})},\lVert R_{2}^{h}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})},\lVert R_{3}^{h}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q_{T})}\leq C_{\Lambda}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\lVert\nabla R_{1}^{h}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{6}(\Omega))}\leq C_{\Lambda}.

(5.18)

To show (
5.12
), we now proceed by performing a priori estimates on the system (
5.13
)–(
5.17
); however, note that, due to the low regularity, these should be done through a proper Faedo-Galerkin discretisation scheme, by passing to the limit. Nevertheless, here we stick to formal estimates to give the idea of the procedure and leave the discretisation details to the interested reader. Indeed, the main estimate is done by testing (
5.13
) by
\psi
, (
5.15
) by
\theta
and summing them up to obtain:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{H}+(\nabla\zeta,\nabla\psi)_{H}+\lVert\nabla\theta\rVert^{2}_{H}=(Q^{h},\psi-\theta)_{H}-(U^{h},\psi)_{H}.

By computing
\nabla\zeta
through equation (
5.14
) and by using the expression of
F^{h}
, hypotheses
A2
and
A3
, Remark
4.3
, (
5.18
) and Hölder, Gagliardo-Nirenberg (
2.1
) and Young’s inequalities, together with Sobolev embeddings, we infer that

\displaystyle(\nabla\zeta,\nabla\psi)_{H}

\displaystyle=((AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})+Ba)\nabla\psi,\nabla\psi)_{H}+(AF^{\prime\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\nabla\overline{\varphi}\,\psi,\nabla\psi)_{H}+(A\nabla R_{1}^{h}(\varphi-\overline{\varphi})^{2},\nabla\psi)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+(AR_{1}^{h}2(\varphi-\overline{\varphi})(\nabla\varphi-\nabla\overline{\varphi}),\nabla\psi)_{H}+(B\nabla a\,\psi,\nabla\psi)_{H}-(B\nabla J\ast\psi,\nabla\psi)_{H}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}-A\lVert F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{3}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-A\lVert\nabla R_{1}^{h}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-2A\lVert R_{1}^{h}\rVert\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\varphi-\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert_{H}-2Bb^{\ast}\lVert\psi\rVert_{H}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert\psi\rVert^{1/2}_{H}\lVert\psi\rVert^{3/2}_{V}-C_{\Lambda}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{V}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-C_{\Lambda}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{V}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert_{H}-C\lVert\psi\rVert_{H}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{0}}{2}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\left(1+\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-C_{\Lambda}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{V}-C_{\Lambda}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{V}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{H^{2}(\Omega)},

where
\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
by Theorem
4.8
. Next, to estimate the right-hand side, we use the definition of
Q^{h}
, together with the local Lipschitz continuity of
P
, Remark
4.3
, (
5.18
), Hölder and Young’s inequalities and the Sobolev embedding
V\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\Omega)
. Indeed, we have that

\displaystyle(Q^{h},\psi-\theta)_{H}

\displaystyle=(P(\overline{\varphi})(\theta-\zeta),\psi-\theta)_{H}+(P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\,\psi,\psi-\theta)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+((P(\varphi)-P(\overline{\varphi}))[(\sigma-\overline{\sigma})-(\mu-\overline{\mu})],\psi-\theta)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+(R_{2}^{h}(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})(\varphi-\overline{\varphi})^{2},\psi-\theta)_{H}

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lvert(\zeta,\psi-\theta)_{H}\rvert+C\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{V}\left(\lVert\sigma-\overline{\sigma}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\mu-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{V}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C_{\Lambda}\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{V},

where
\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
by Theorem
4.8
.
Moreover, to estimate the remaining term
\lvert(\zeta,\psi-\theta)_{H}\rvert
, we use (
5.14
) and similar techniques to see that

\displaystyle\lvert(\zeta,\psi-\theta)_{H}\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert(AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\psi,\psi)_{H}+(AR_{1}^{h}(\varphi-\overline{\varphi})^{2},\psi)_{H}+(Ba\psi,\psi)_{H}-B(J\ast\psi,\psi)_{H}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C_{\Lambda}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{H}.

Finally, for the last term we use the local Lipschitz continuity of
\mathbbm{h}
, Remark
4.3
and (
5.18
), together with the embedding
V\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\Omega)
and Hölder and Young’s inequalities, yielding

\displaystyle(U^{h},\psi)_{H}

\displaystyle=(\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\psi\overline{u}+(\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi}))h+R_{2}^{h}(\varphi-\overline{\varphi})^{2}\overline{u},\psi)_{H}

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert\mathbbm{h}(\varphi)-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert h\rVert_{H}\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{4}(\Omega)}+C\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\lVert\psi\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{0}}{4}\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{V}+C\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{H}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{V}+C\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{V}.

Therefore, by putting all together and integrating on
(0,t)
, for any
t\in(0,T)
, we arrive at the inequality:

\displaystyle\lVert\psi(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert\theta(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\theta\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\left(1+\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{V}\,\mathrm{d}t+C\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;V)}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\sigma-\overline{\sigma}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{V}+\lVert\mu-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{V}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;V)}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert h\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;V)}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Next, we apply the continuous dependence result given by Theorem
4.11
on the terms depending on the differences between
(\varphi,\mu,\sigma)
and
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})
, to deduce that

\displaystyle\lVert\psi(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert\theta(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{c_{0}}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\theta\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C\int_{0}^{T}\left(1+\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\lVert h\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}+C\lVert k\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}.

Then, by applying Gronwall’s lemma, we obtain the estimate:

\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}+\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}\leq C\lVert h\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}+C\lVert k\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}.

(5.19)

Moreover, by comparison in (
5.14
) and by using (
5.19
), we can easily see that also

\lVert\zeta\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)}\leq C\lVert h\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}+C\lVert k\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}.

(5.20)

Additionally, by testing (
5.14
) by
-\Delta\zeta
, integrating by parts and performing similar estimates to the ones done when studying
(\nabla\zeta,\nabla\psi)_{H}
above, we can infer that

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\zeta\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert\nabla\zeta\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\left(1+\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\nabla\psi\rVert^{2}_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{V}+C\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{V}\lVert\varphi-\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{2}_{H^{2}(\Omega)}.

Hence, by integrating on
(0,T)
and using (
5.19
) and Theorem
4.11
, we obtain that

\lVert\zeta\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;V)}\leq C\lVert h\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}+C\lVert k\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}.

(5.21)

Finally, by comparison in (
5.13
) and (
5.15
) and by using (
5.19
) and (
5.21
), we can also easily infer that

\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})}+\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})}\leq C\lVert h\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}+C\lVert k\rVert^{4}_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}.

(5.22)

Therefore, by putting together (
5.19
), (
5.20
), (
5.21
) and (
5.22
), we realise that we have actually shown (
5.12
) with
s=4>2
; thus the proof is concluded.
∎

5.3
Adjoint system and optimality conditions

In order to write down the necessary conditions of optimality in a form which is suitable for applications, we now introduce the adjoint system to the optimal control problem (CP). Indeed, we fix an optimal state
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})=\mathcal{S}(\overline{u},\overline{v})
. Then, by using the formal Lagrangian method with adjoint variables
(p,q,r)
, one can find that the adjoint system, which is formally solved by these variables, has the following form:

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}p+AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})q+Baq-BJ\ast q-P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})(p-r)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+p\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}=\alpha_{Q}(\overline{\varphi}-\varphi_{Q})\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(5.23)

\displaystyle-q-\Delta p+P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r)=0\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(5.24)

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}r-\Delta r-P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r)=\beta_{Q}(\overline{\sigma}-\sigma_{Q})\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }Q_{T},

(5.25)

together with the following boundary and final conditions:

\displaystyle\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}p=\partial_{\boldsymbol{n}}r=0\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{on }\Sigma_{T},

(5.26)

\displaystyle p(T)=\alpha_{\Omega}(\overline{\varphi}(T)-\varphi_{\Omega}),\hskip 10.00002ptr(T)=\beta_{\Omega}(\overline{\sigma}(T)-\sigma_{\Omega})\hskip 20.00003pt

\displaystyle\text{in }\Omega.

(5.27)

First, we prove the well-posedness of this adjoint system in the following theorem.

Theorem 5.7
.

Assume hypotheses
A1
–
A7
,
B1
–
B2
and
C1
–
C5
. Let
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})\in\mathbb{X}
be the strong solution to (
1.7
)–(
1.11
), corresponding to
(\overline{u},\overline{v})\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
. Then, the adjoint system (
5.23
)–(
5.27
) admits a unique weak solution such that

\displaystyle p\in H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V),

\displaystyle q\in L^{2}(0,T;V^{*}),

\displaystyle r\in H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V),

which fulfils (
5.23
)–(
5.27
) in variational formulation, i.e. it satisfies

\displaystyle\langle-\partial_{t}p,w\rangle_{V}+\langle q,(AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})+Ba)w\rangle_{V}-B\langle q,J\ast w\rangle_{V}-(P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})(p-r),w)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+(p\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u},w)_{H}=(\alpha_{Q}(\overline{\varphi}-\varphi_{Q}),w)_{H}

(5.28)

\displaystyle\langle q,w\rangle_{V}=(\nabla p,\nabla w)_{H}+(P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r),w)_{H}

(5.29)

\displaystyle\langle-\partial_{t}r,w\rangle_{V}+(\nabla r,\nabla w)_{H}-(P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r),w)_{H}=(\beta_{Q}(\overline{\sigma}-\sigma_{Q}),w)_{H}

(5.30)

for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
and for any
w\in V
.

Remark 5.8
.

When writing the variational formulation (
5.28
), we used hypothesis
A2
on the symmetry of the kernel
J
to move the convolution operator onto the test function. Indeed, by a simple change of variables, one can formally see that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(J\ast q)\,w\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{\Omega}J(x-y)q(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\right)w(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{\Omega}J(y-x)w(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right)q(y)\,\mathrm{d}y=\int_{\Omega}q\,(J\ast w)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

We observe that (
5.23
)–(
5.27
) is a backward linear parabolic system, therefore it is not difficult to prove the existence of a solution through a Faedo-Galerkin discretisation scheme. Here we proceed with formal a priori estimates for the sake of brevity. Indeed, for the main estimate, we test (
5.28
) with
p
, (
5.30
) with
r
and we sum them up to obtain:

\begin{split}&-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert p\rVert^{2}_{H}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{H}+\langle q,(AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})+Ba)p\rangle_{V}-B\langle q,J\ast p\rangle_{V}+\lVert\nabla r\rVert^{2}_{H}\\
&\quad=(P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r),r)_{H}+(P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})(p-r),p)_{H}+(p\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u},p)_{H}\\
&\qquad+(\alpha_{Q}(\overline{\varphi}-\varphi_{Q}),p)_{H}+(\beta_{Q}(\overline{\sigma}-\sigma_{Q}),r)_{H}.\end{split}

(5.31)

Next, we estimate the two terms involving the duality with
q
by using equation (
5.29
), together with hypotheses
A2
and
A3
, Remark
4.3
and Hölder, Gagliardo-Nirenberg (
2.1
) and Young’s inequalities. Indeed, we infer that

\displaystyle\langle q,(AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})+Ba)p\rangle_{V}-B\langle q,J\ast p\rangle_{V}=

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=(\nabla p,(AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})+Ba)\nabla p)_{H}+(\nabla p,(AF^{\prime\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\nabla\overline{\varphi}+B\nabla a)p)_{H}+(P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r),p)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-B(\nabla p,\nabla J\ast p)_{H}-B(P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r),J\ast p)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\geq c_{0}\lVert\nabla p\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert\nabla p\rVert_{H}\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert p\rVert_{L^{3}(\Omega)}-2Bb^{\ast}\lVert\nabla p\rVert_{H}\lVert p\rVert_{H}-C(\lVert p\rVert_{H}+\lVert r\rVert_{H})\lVert p\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\geq c_{0}\lVert\nabla p\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\lVert p\rVert^{1/2}_{H}\lVert p\rVert^{3/2}_{V}-2Bb^{\ast}\lVert\nabla p\rVert_{H}\lVert p\rVert_{H}-C(\lVert p\rVert_{H}+\lVert r\rVert_{H})\lVert p\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\geq\frac{c_{0}}{2}\lVert\nabla p\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\left(1+\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert p\rVert^{2}_{H}-C\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{H},

where
\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
by Theorem
4.8
and Sobolev embeddings. Moreover, we also estimate the right-hand side of (
5.31
) by using Remark
4.3
, hypothesis
C2
and Cauchy-Schwarz and Young’s inequalities as follows:

\displaystyle(P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r),r)_{H}+(P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})(p-r),p)_{H}+(p\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u},p)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+(\alpha_{Q}(\overline{\varphi}-\varphi_{Q}),p)_{H}+(\beta_{Q}(\overline{\sigma}-\sigma_{Q}),r)_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq C\left(1+\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert p\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\alpha_{Q}(\overline{\varphi}-\varphi_{Q})\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\lVert\beta_{Q}(\overline{\sigma}-\sigma_{Q})\rVert^{2}_{H},

where
\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T)
by Theorem
4.8
.
Then, by putting all together and integrating on
(t,T)
, for any
t\in(0,T)
, from (
5.31
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lVert p(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{1}{2}\lVert r(t)\rVert^{2}_{H}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{t}^{T}\lVert\nabla p\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{T}\lVert\nabla r\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\lVert\alpha_{\Omega}(\overline{\varphi}(T)-\varphi_{\Omega})\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert\beta_{\Omega}(\overline{\sigma}(T)-\sigma_{\Omega})\rVert^{2}_{H}+C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\int_{0}^{T}\left(1+\lVert\nabla\overline{\varphi}\rVert^{4}_{L^{6}(\Omega)}+\lVert\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu}\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\right)\lVert p\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\alpha_{Q}(\overline{\varphi}-\varphi_{Q})\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\beta_{Q}(\overline{\sigma}-\sigma_{Q})\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Therefore, by using Gronwall’s lemma, together with hypothesis
C2
, we conclude that

\begin{split}&\lVert p\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}+\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H)\cap L^{2}(0,T;V)}\\
&\quad\leq C\Big(\lVert\alpha_{\Omega}(\overline{\varphi}(T)-\varphi_{\Omega})\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert\beta_{\Omega}(\overline{\sigma}(T)-\sigma_{\Omega})\rVert^{2}_{H}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\alpha_{Q}(\overline{\varphi}-\varphi_{Q})\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\beta_{Q}(\overline{\sigma}-\sigma_{Q})\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big).\end{split}

(5.32)

Moreover, by comparison in (
5.28
), (
5.29
) and (
5.30
), we also easily see that (
5.32
) implies the following estimate:

\begin{split}&\lVert p\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})}+\lVert q\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;V^{*})}+\lVert r\rVert^{2}_{H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})}\\
&\quad\leq C\Big(\lVert\alpha_{\Omega}(\overline{\varphi}(T)-\varphi_{\Omega})\rVert^{2}_{H}+\lVert\beta_{\Omega}(\overline{\sigma}(T)-\sigma_{\Omega})\rVert^{2}_{H}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\alpha_{Q}(\overline{\varphi}-\varphi_{Q})\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\beta_{Q}(\overline{\sigma}-\sigma_{Q})\rVert^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big).\end{split}

(5.33)

All these estimates can, then, be repeated in a proper discretisation framework and, by passing to the limit, one can prove the existence of a solution with the sought regularities. Moreover, being the system linear, estimates (
5.32
) and (
5.33
) also imply the uniqueness of the solution. This concludes the proof of Theorem
5.7
.
∎

To conclude, with the adjoint variables, we can finally determine and then simplify the first-order necessary conditions. Indeed, we have the following result:

Theorem 5.9
.

Assume hypotheses
A1
–
A5
,
B1
–
B2
and
C1
–
C5
. Let
(\overline{u},\overline{v})\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
be an optimal control for
(CP)
and let
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})=\mathcal{S}(\overline{u},\overline{v})\in\mathbb{X}
be the corresponding optimal state, i.e. the solution of (
1.7
)–(
1.11
) with such
(\overline{u},\overline{v})
. Let also
(p,q,r)
be the adjoint variables to
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\sigma},\overline{\mu})
, i.e. the solutions to the adjoint system (
5.23
)–(
5.27
). Then, they satisfy the following variational inequality, which holds for any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
:

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(-\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})p+\alpha_{u}\overline{u})(u-\overline{u})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(r+\beta_{v}\overline{v})(v-\overline{v})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0.

(5.34)

Proof.

First observe that the cost functional
\mathcal{J}
is convex and Fréchet-differentiable in the space
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\times\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)\times L^{2}(Q_{T})\times L^{2}(Q_{T})
. Next, in Theorem
5.5
we showed that the solution operator
\mathcal{S}
is Fréchet-differentiable from
\mathcal{U}_{R}\times\mathcal{V}_{R}\subseteq L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2}
to
\mathbb{W}
. Consequently, since by standard results
L^{2}(0,T;V)\cap H^{1}(0,T;V^{*})
is embedded with continuity in
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H)
, we also have that the operator
(\mathcal{S}_{1},\mathcal{S}_{3})
that selects the first and third components of
\mathcal{S}
is Fréchet-differentiable from
\mathcal{U}_{R}\times\mathcal{V}_{R}
to
(\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H))^{2}
. Therefore, we can consider the
reduced cost functional

f:L^{\infty}(Q_{T})^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
, defined as

f(u,v):=\mathcal{J}(\mathcal{S}_{1}(u,v),\mathcal{S}_{3}(u,v),u,v),

which, by the chain rule, is Fréchet-differentiable in
\mathcal{U}_{R}\times\mathcal{V}_{R}
.

At this point, we can rewrite our optimal control problem (CP) through the reduced cost functional as the minimisation problem

\argmin_{(u,v)\in\,\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}}f(u,v).

Then, if
(\overline{u},\overline{v})
is optimal, since
\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
is convex and
f
is Fréchet-differentiable, it has to satisfy the necessary optimality condition

f^{\prime}(\overline{u},\overline{v})[(u-\overline{u},v-\overline{v})]\geq 0\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for any }(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}.

Hence, by computing explicitly the derivative of
f
, we get that for any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\alpha_{\Omega}(\overline{\varphi}(T)-\varphi_{\Omega})\xi(T)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\alpha_{Q}(\overline{\varphi}-\varphi_{Q})\xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\beta_{\Omega}(\overline{\sigma}(T)-\sigma_{\Omega})\rho(T)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\beta_{Q}(\overline{\sigma}-\sigma_{Q})\rho\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\alpha_{u}\overline{u}(u-\overline{u})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\beta_{v}\overline{v}(v-\overline{v})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0,

where
\xi=D\mathcal{S}_{1}(\overline{u},\overline{v})[u-\overline{u},v-\overline{v}]
and
\rho=D\mathcal{S}_{3}(\overline{u},\overline{v})[u-\overline{u},v-\overline{v}]
are the components of the solution
(\xi,\eta,\rho)
to the linearised system (
5.2
)–(
5.6
) in
(\overline{\varphi},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})
corresponding to
h=u-\overline{u}
and
k=v-\overline{v}
. Note that in what follows we are going to write all the integral terms as if they were in strong form, however keep in mind that, in our regularity setting, all products involving time-derivatives, laplacians and
q
have to be intended as duality products.

Now observe that the right-hand sides and the final conditions of the adjoint system appear in this inequality, therefore by substituting equations (
5.23
), (
5.25
) and (
5.27
) in the previous expression, we find that for any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}p(T)\xi(T)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\Big(-\partial_{t}p+AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})q+Baq-BJ\ast q

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})(p-r)+p\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}\Big)\xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\Omega}r(T)\rho(T)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big(-\partial_{t}r-\Delta r-P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r)\big)\rho\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\alpha_{u}\overline{u}(u-\overline{u})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\beta_{v}\overline{v}(v-\overline{v})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0.

Now we integrate by parts in time, by using also the initial conditions (
5.6
) on the linearised system, and in space, by using the boundary conditions (
5.26
) and (
5.5
), and, after cancellations, we find that equivalently for any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\Big(p\xi_{t}+AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\xi q+Ba\xi q-B(J\ast\xi)q-P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})(p-r)\xi+p\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}\,\xi\Big)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big(\rho_{t}r-\Delta\rho\,r-P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r)\rho\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\alpha_{u}\overline{u}(u-\overline{u})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\beta_{v}\overline{v}(v-\overline{v})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0,

where we also used the symmetry of the kernel
J
. By factoring out
p
,
q
and
r
respectively, we can rewrite the previous inequality as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}p\left(\xi_{t}-P(\overline{\varphi})\rho-P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi+\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}\,\xi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\left(AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\xi+Ba\xi-BJ\ast\xi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}r\left(\rho_{t}-\Delta\rho+P(\overline{\varphi})\rho+P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\alpha_{u}\overline{u}(u-\overline{u})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\beta_{v}\overline{v}(v-\overline{v})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0.

Finally, we use equation (
5.24
) and again integration by parts to also get that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(-q-\Delta p+P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r)\right)\eta\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}-\eta q-\Delta\eta\,p+P(\overline{\varphi})(p-r)\eta\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

Then, by adding this to the previous inequality, we at last infer that for any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}p\left(\xi_{t}-\Delta\eta-P(\overline{\varphi})(\rho-\eta)-P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi+\mathbbm{h}^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\overline{u}\,\xi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\left(-\eta+AF^{\prime\prime}(\overline{\varphi})\xi+Ba\xi-BJ\ast\xi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}r\left(\rho_{t}-\Delta\rho+P(\overline{\varphi})(\rho-\eta)+P^{\prime}(\overline{\varphi})(\overline{\sigma}-\overline{\mu})\xi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\alpha_{u}\overline{u}(u-\overline{u})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\beta_{v}\overline{v}(v-\overline{v})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0.

To conclude, we notice that the expressions enclosed in the parentheses are exactly the equations (
5.2
), (
5.3
), (
5.4
) of the linearised system, up to their source terms. Hence, by substituting those into our inequality, we find that for any
(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}-p\,\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})(u-\overline{u})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}r(v-\overline{v})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\alpha_{u}\overline{u}(u-\overline{u})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\beta_{v}\overline{v}(v-\overline{v})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0,

which is exactly (
5.34
). This concludes the proof of Theorem
5.9
.
∎

Remark 5.10
.

Observe that, since
\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
is closed and convex, (
5.34
) means that, if
\alpha_{u}>0
and
\beta_{v}>0
, the optimal control
(\overline{u},\overline{v})
is exactly the
L^{2}(Q_{T})^{2}
-orthogonal projection of
(\alpha_{u}^{-1}\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})\,p,-\beta_{v}^{-1}r)
onto
\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
.
In particular, it can be shown that, due to the structure of
\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
, the above
L^{2}(Q_{T})^{2}
-projection has the explicit form:

\displaystyle\overline{u}(x,t)=\min\left\{u_{\text{max}}(x,t),\max\left\{\alpha_{u}^{-1}\mathbbm{h}(\overline{\varphi})\,p(x,t),u_{\text{min}}(x,t)\right\}\right\}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for a.e. }(x,t)\in Q_{T},

\displaystyle\overline{v}(x,t)=\min\left\{v_{\text{max}}(x,t),\max\left\{-\beta_{v}^{-1}r(x,t),v_{\text{min}}(x,t)\right\}\right\}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for a.e. }(x,t)\in Q_{T}.

To get this kind of explicit form, it is crucial for
\mathcal{U}_{\text{ad}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\text{ad}}
to be described by box constraints in
L^{\infty}(Q_{T})
.
We stress that our new maximal regularity strategy allowed us to get highly regular solutions by only assuming such constraints on the controls.
In this sense, the result presented here can be thought as a partial improvement to the optimality conditions proved in
[
17
]
, where we also needed the additional
H^{1}(0,T;H)
-regularity on the control
u
.
